W:{\mathcal{H}}\to{\mathcal{H}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{2}

such that

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits W\circ\pi=\pi_{1}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{2}

and
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W\right)(\Gamma)=\Gamma_{1}\otimes\Gamma_{2}
.
Because
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits W\left(\pi(\bar{h})\right)

commutes with
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits W\left(\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}}\right)\right)={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{1})\otimes\mathbb{I}

and
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W\right)(\Gamma)=\Gamma_{1}\otimes\Gamma_{2}
,

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits W\left(\pi(\bar{h})\right)
is of the form

\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{1}}\otimes x

with
x\in{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{2})_{+,1}
such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\Gamma_{2}(x)=x
.
Because
({\mathcal{H}}_{2},\pi_{2},\Gamma_{2})
is an irreducible covariant representation,
as in the proof of Lemma 4.9
[
O1
]
,
there is some
\hat{h}\in\left({\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{0}}\right)_{1,+}

such that

\left\|\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{1}}\otimes\pi_{2}(\hat{h})-\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{1}}\otimes x\right)W\left(\xi+\eta\right)\right\|
and

\left\|\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{1}}\otimes\pi_{2}(\hat{h})-\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{1}}\otimes x\right)W\left(\xi-\eta\right)\right\|

are small enough so that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\pi(\hat{h})(\xi+\eta)\right\|\leq\left\|\left(\pi(\hat{h})-\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(\bar{h})\right)\left(\xi+\eta\right)\right\|+\left\|\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(\bar{h})(\xi+\eta)\right\|\\
&=\left\|\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{1}}\otimes\pi_{2}(\hat{h})-\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{1}}\otimes x\right)W\left(\xi+\eta\right)\right\|+\left\|\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(\bar{h})(\xi+\eta)\right\|<\frac{\delta_{2,a}(\frac{\epsilon}{8})e^{-\pi}}{4\sqrt{2}},\\
&\left\|\left(\mathbb{I}-\pi(\hat{h})\right)(\xi-\eta)\right\|\leq\left\|\left(\pi(\hat{h})-\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(\bar{h})\right)\left(\xi-\eta\right)\right\|+\left\|\left(\mathbb{I}-\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(\bar{h})\right)(\xi-\eta)\right\|\\
&=\left\|\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{1}}\otimes\pi_{2}(\hat{h})-\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{1}}\otimes x\right)W\left(\xi-\eta\right)\right\|+\left\|\left(\mathbb{I}-\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(\bar{h})\right)(\xi-\eta)\right\|<\frac{\delta_{2,a}(\frac{\epsilon}{8})e^{-\pi}}{4\sqrt{2}},\end{split}

(4.26)

hold.
Then by the argument in Lemma 4.9 of
[
O1
]
(eq. (91)),
for this
\hat{h}
,
we obtain (
4.23
).

\square

Lemma 4.9
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
and a finite subset
{\mathcal{F}}

of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
, set

\tilde{\delta}_{30}\left(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}\right):=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\delta}_{28}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4},\Lambda(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})\right)

and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}\left(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}\right):={\mathcal{G}}_{\Lambda(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})}
,
where
\tilde{\delta}_{28}(\varepsilon,\Lambda_{0})
is defined in Lemma
4.8
.
Then for any irreducible covariant representation
({\mathcal{H}},\pi,\Gamma)
of
\Sigma_{\Lambda}

with the associated decomposition
({\mathcal{H}}_{\pm},\pi_{\pm})

and unit vectors
\xi,\eta\in{\mathcal{H}}_{+}

such that

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\tilde{\xi},\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(xy^{*})\tilde{\xi}\right\rangle-\left\langle\tilde{\eta},\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(xy^{*})\tilde{\eta}\right\rangle\right|<\tilde{\delta}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}),\quad x,y\in\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}).

(4.27)

for
\tilde{\xi}:=\xi\oplus 0
,
\tilde{\eta}:=\eta\oplus 0\in{\mathcal{H}}_{+}\oplus{\mathcal{H}}_{-}
,
there is a continuous map
v:[0,1]\to{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)})

satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tilde{\eta}=\pi\left(v(1)\right)\tilde{\xi},\quad v(0)=\mathbb{I},\\
&\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits v(t)(a)-a\right\|<\varepsilon,\quad a\in{\mathcal{F}}.\end{split}

(4.28)

Let
{\mathcal{K}}
be a finite dimensional subspace of
{\mathcal{H}}

spanned by

\left\{\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(xy^{*})\tilde{\xi},\;\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(xy^{*})\tilde{\eta}\mid x,y\in\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})\right\}.

Then because
C^{*}(\Sigma_{\Lambda})
is simple,
by Glimm’s Lemma (Lemma 5.2.5
[
F
]
), there exists a unit vector
\zeta\in{\mathcal{K}}^{\perp}

such that

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\tilde{\xi},\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(xy^{*})\tilde{\xi}\right\rangle-\left\langle\zeta,\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(xy^{*})\zeta\right\rangle\right|<\tilde{\delta}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})<\tilde{\delta}_{28}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4},\Lambda(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})\right),\quad x,y\in\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}).

(4.29)

Combining this with (
4.27
),we also have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\tilde{\eta},\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(xy^{*})\tilde{\eta}\right\rangle-\left\langle\zeta,\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(xy^{*})\zeta\right\rangle\right|<2\tilde{\delta}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})=\tilde{\delta}_{28}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4},\Lambda(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})\right),\quad x,y\in\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}).

(4.30)

Because we have

\displaystyle\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(y^{*})\tilde{\xi},\;\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(y^{*})\tilde{\eta}\perp\left(\pi\times\Gamma\right)(x^{*})\zeta\;\;\text{for any}\;\;x,y\in\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}),

(4.31)

from Lemma
4.8
,
there are
h_{1},h_{2}\in\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})}^{(0)}\right)_{1,+}

such that

\displaystyle\left\|e^{i\pi\pi(h_{1})}\tilde{\xi}-\zeta\right\|,\left\|e^{i\pi\pi(h_{2})}\tilde{\eta}-\zeta\right\|<\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}\delta_{2,a}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{32}\right).

(4.32)

From this, recalling that
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
are even,
we have

\displaystyle\left\|e^{i\pi\pi_{+}(h_{1})}\xi-e^{i\pi\pi_{+}(h_{2})}\eta\right\|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{+}}=\left\|e^{i\pi\pi(h_{1})}\tilde{\xi}-e^{i\pi\pi(h_{2})}\tilde{\eta}\right\|<\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\delta_{2,a}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{32}\right).

(4.33)

Here
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{+}}
means
the norm associated to
{\mathcal{H}}_{+}
.
Because
\pi_{+}
is irreducible, by the argument in the proof of Lemma 4.12 in
[
O1
]

(around equation (103)),
there is some self-adjoint
K\in{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}e^{i\pi_{+}(K)}e^{i\pi\pi_{+}(h_{1})}\xi=e^{i\pi\pi_{+}(h_{2})}\eta,\\
\left\|K\right\|<\delta_{1,a}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{32}\right),\quad\sup_{\theta\in[0,1]}\left\|e^{iK\theta}-\mathbb{I}\right\|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{32}.\end{split}

(4.34)

Here,
\delta_{1,a}(\varepsilon)
is given in Notation B.3 of
[
O1
]
.
From this, as in the proof of Lemma 4.12
[
O1
]
(from equation (105)),
we can find
v(t)

satisfying the condition (
4.28
).

\square

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{i},\pi_{i},\Gamma_{i})
,
i=0,1
be
irreducible covariant representations of

\Sigma_{\Lambda}
.
Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{i\pm},\pi_{i\pm})

be the decomposition associated to
({\mathcal{H}}_{i},\pi_{i},\Gamma_{i})
,
and
\xi_{i}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{i+}
a unit vector.
Set
\tilde{\xi}_{i}:=\xi_{i}\oplus 0\in{\mathcal{H}}_{i+}\oplus{\mathcal{H}}_{i-}
.
Suppose that for
\varepsilon>0
and a finite set
{\mathcal{F}}\subset\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)_{1}
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\tilde{\xi}_{0},\left(\pi_{0}\times\Gamma_{0}\right)(xy^{*})\tilde{\xi}_{0}\right\rangle-\left\langle\tilde{\xi}_{1},\left(\pi_{1}\times\Gamma_{1}\right)(xy^{*})\tilde{\xi}_{1}\right\rangle\right|<\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\delta}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}),\quad x,y\in\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})

(4.35)

hold.
Then for any
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
and finite set
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\subset C^{*}(\Sigma)
,
there exists a norm continuous path

v:[0,1]\to{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)})

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|\left\langle\tilde{\xi}_{0},\left(\pi_{0}\times\Gamma_{0}\right)(a)\tilde{\xi}_{0}\right\rangle-\left\langle\tilde{\xi}_{1},\left(\pi_{1}\times\Gamma_{1}\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v(1)\right)(a)\tilde{\xi}_{1}\right\rangle\right|<\varepsilon^{\prime},\quad a\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},\\
&\left\|\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v(t)\right)(y)-y\right\|<\varepsilon,\quad y\in{\mathcal{F}},\;t\in[0,1].\end{split}

(4.36)

This corresponds to Lemma 4.14 of
[
O1
]
.
By Lemma
4.7
, there exists a self-adjoint
h\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}

such that

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\tilde{\xi}_{0},\left(\pi_{0}\times\Gamma_{0}\right)(a)\tilde{\xi}_{0}\right\rangle-\left\langle\tilde{\xi}_{1},\left(\pi_{1}\times\Gamma_{1}\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(e^{ih}\right)(a)\tilde{\xi}_{1}\right\rangle\right|<\min\{\varepsilon^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\delta}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})\},\quad a\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\cup\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})\left(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})\right)^{*}.

(4.37)

Combining this with (
4.35
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\tilde{\xi}_{1},\left(\pi_{1}\times\Gamma_{1}\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(e^{ih}\right)(xy^{*})\tilde{\xi}_{1}\right\rangle-\left\langle\tilde{\xi}_{1},\left(\pi_{1}\times\Gamma_{1}\right)(xy^{*})\tilde{\xi}_{1}\right\rangle\right|<\tilde{\delta}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}),\quad x,y\in\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}).

(4.38)

Here, because
h
is even,
we have

\displaystyle\left(\pi_{1}\times\Gamma_{1}\right)\left(e^{-ih}\right)\tilde{\xi}_{1}=e^{-i\pi_{1+}(h)}\xi_{1}\oplus 0.

(4.39)

From this, applying Lemma
4.9
,
we obtain the Lemma.

\square

Having Lemma
4.7
and Lemma
4.10
, the proof of Proposition
4.6

is the same as that of Proposition 4.2 of
[
O1
]
.

\square

Next we prepare a proposition needed for the case (b) of Proposition
4.5
.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{i},\pi_{i})
,
i=0,1
be irreducible representations of

{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
such that
\pi_{i}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{i})
.
Let
\xi_{i}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
,
i=0,1
be unit vectors
and set

\displaystyle\omega_{i}:=\left\langle\xi_{i},\pi_{i}(\cdot)\xi_{i}\right\rangle.

(4.40)

Then there is an automorphism
\eta\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})

such that

\displaystyle\omega_{1}=\omega_{0}\eta.

(4.41)

The proof follows the argument in
[
KOS
]
,
choosing the unitary there each time from
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}
,
which is possible because of
\pi_{i}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{i})
.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{i},\pi_{i})
,
i=0,1
be irreducible representations

{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
such that
\pi_{i}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{i})
.
Let
\xi_{i}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
,
i=0,1
be unit vectors.
Then for any finite subset
{\mathcal{F}}
of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
and
\varepsilon>0
,
there exists a self-adjoint element
h\in{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}_{\Lambda}

such that

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\xi_{0},\pi_{0}(a)\xi_{0}\right\rangle-\left\langle\xi_{1},\pi_{1}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(e^{ih})(a)\xi_{1}\right\rangle\right|<\varepsilon,\quad a\in{\mathcal{F}}.

(4.42)

Let
\omega_{i}:=\left\langle\xi_{i},\pi_{i}(\cdot)\xi_{i}\right\rangle

i=0,1

be pure states on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
.
By Lemma B.1
[
O1
]
,
there exist
f\in\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)_{+1}
and
a unit vector
\xi_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{1}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pi_{1}(f)\xi_{1}^{\prime}=\xi_{1}^{\prime},\\
\left\|f\left(a-\omega_{0}(a)f\right)\right\|<\varepsilon,\quad a\in{\mathcal{F}}.\end{split}

(4.43)

Because we now have
\pi_{1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{1})
,
for unit vectors
\xi_{1},\xi_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
there exists
a self-adjoint element
h\in{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}_{\Lambda}

such that
e^{i\pi_{1}(h)}\xi_{1}^{\prime}=\xi_{1}

by the Kadison transitivity.
For this
h
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\left\langle\xi_{0},\pi_{0}(a)\xi_{0}\right\rangle-\left\langle\xi_{1},\pi_{1}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(e^{ih})(a)\xi_{1}\right\rangle\right|=\left|\left\langle\xi_{1}^{\prime},\pi_{1}(f)\left(\omega_{0}(a)-\pi_{1}(a)\right)\pi_{1}(f)\xi_{1}^{\prime}\right\rangle\right|<\varepsilon,\quad a\in{\mathcal{F}}.\end{split}

(4.44)

\square

Lemma 4.13
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
and a finite subset
\Lambda_{0}\subset\Lambda
,
there exists
\tilde{\delta}_{28}(\varepsilon,\Lambda_{0})>0

satisfying the following:
For any
({\mathcal{H}},\pi)
an irreducible representation of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}

with
\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}^{(0)}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})

and unit vectors
\xi,\eta\in{\mathcal{H}}

satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pi(x^{*})\xi\perp\pi(y^{*})\eta,\\
\left|\left\langle\xi,\pi(xy^{*})\xi\right\rangle-\left\langle\eta,\pi(xy^{*})\eta\right\rangle\right|<\tilde{\delta}_{28}(\varepsilon,\Lambda_{0}),\\
\text{for any }\quad x,y\in\{e_{II_{0}}^{(\Lambda_{0})}\}_{I},\end{split}

(4.45)

there exist self-adjoint operators
h\in\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}\right)_{+1}

and
k\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bar{h}:=\sum_{I}e_{II_{0}}^{(\Lambda_{0})}he_{I_{0}I}^{(\Lambda_{0})}\in\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{0}}^{(0)}\right)_{+1},\\
\left\|e^{i\pi\pi(\bar{h})}\xi-\eta\right\|<\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\delta_{2,a}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\right)\\
\left\|e^{itk}-\mathbb{I}\right\|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8},\quad t\in[0,1],\\
e^{i\pi(k)}e^{i\pi\pi(\bar{h})}\xi=\eta.\end{split}

(4.46)

With
\delta_{3,a}(\varepsilon,n)
given in Lemma B.6
[
O1
]
,
we set

\tilde{\delta}_{28}(\varepsilon,\Lambda_{0}):=\delta_{3,a}(\varepsilon,2^{d|\Lambda_{0}|})
.
Suppose that
({\mathcal{H}},\pi)
is an irreducible representation of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}

satisfying the conditions above with respect to this
\tilde{\delta}_{28}(\varepsilon,\Lambda_{0})
.
Applying Lemma B.6
[
O1
]
, we obtain

h\in\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)_{+,1}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|\bar{h}\left(\xi+\eta\right)\right\|<\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}\delta_{2,a}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\right)e^{-\pi},\\
\left\|\left(\mathbb{I}-\bar{h}\right)\left(\xi-\eta\right)\right\|<\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}\delta_{2,a}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\right)e^{-\pi}\end{split}

(4.47)

holds for

\displaystyle\bar{h}:=\sum_{I}e_{II_{0}}^{(\Lambda_{0})}he_{I_{0}I}^{(\Lambda_{0})}.

(4.48)

This
h
is obtained via the Kadison transitivity, using the irreducibility of
\pi

(see section 3 of
[
KOS
]
, also 5.6.1 of
[
F
]
).
Because we have
\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
,
in fact we can choose this
h
from
\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}\right)_{+,1}
.
Doing so, we have

\displaystyle\bar{h}:=\sum_{I}e_{II_{0}}^{(\Lambda_{0})}he_{I_{0}I}^{(\Lambda_{0})}\in\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}\right)_{+1}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}}^{\prime}\subset\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_{0}}^{(0)}\right)_{+1}.

(4.49)

The last inclusion comes from Lemma 4.15
[
AM
]
.
For this
\bar{h}
,
by the argument in Lemma 4.9 of
[
O1
]
(eq. (91)),
we have

\displaystyle\left\|e^{i\pi\pi(\bar{h})}\xi-\eta\right\|<\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\delta_{2,a}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\right).

(4.50)

Because we have
\pi\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
,
by the Kadison transitivity, (Theorem B.4
[
O1
]
)
we get some self-adjoint
k\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}

such that

\displaystyle e^{i\pi(k)}e^{i\pi\pi(\bar{h})}\xi=\eta,\quad\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|e^{ikt}-\mathbb{I}\right\|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8}.

(4.51)

\square

Lemma 4.14
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
and a finite subset
{\mathcal{F}}

of
\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)_{1}
, set

\tilde{\delta}_{30}\left(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}\right):=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\delta}_{28}\left(\epsilon,\Lambda(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})\right)

and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}\left(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}\right):={\mathcal{G}}_{\Lambda(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})}
,
where
\tilde{\delta}_{28}\left(\epsilon,\Lambda_{0}\right)
is defined in Lemma
4.13
.
Then for any irreducible representation
({\mathcal{H}},\pi)
of

{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
with
\pi({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)})^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
,
and unit vectors
\xi,\eta\in{\mathcal{H}}

such that

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\xi,\pi(xy^{*})\xi\right\rangle-\left\langle\eta,\pi(xy^{*})\eta\right\rangle\right|<\tilde{\delta}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}),\quad x,y\in\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}\left(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}\right),

(4.52)

there exists a norm-continuous path
v:[0,1]\to{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)})

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\eta=\pi\left(v(1)\right)\xi,\quad v(0)=\mathbb{I},\\
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v(t)\right)(a)-a\right\|<\varepsilon,\quad a\in{\mathcal{F}}.\end{split}

(4.53)

Because
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
is simple, by Glimm’s Lemma, there exists a unit vector
\zeta

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\zeta\perp\pi(xy^{*})\xi,\pi(xy^{*})\eta,\quad x,y\in\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}),\\
\left|\left\langle\xi,\pi(xy^{*})\xi\right\rangle-\left\langle\zeta,\pi(xy^{*})\zeta\right\rangle\right|<\tilde{\delta}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})<\tilde{\delta}_{28}(\varepsilon,\Lambda(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})),\quad x,y\in\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}).\end{split}

(4.54)

From the latter inequality combined with (
4.52
),
we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\eta,\pi(xy^{*})\eta\right\rangle-\left\langle\zeta,\pi(xy^{*})\zeta\right\rangle\right|<\tilde{\delta}_{28}(\varepsilon,\Lambda(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})).

(4.55)

By Lemma
4.13
,
there exist
h_{1},h_{2}\in\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\setminus\Lambda(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})}^{(0)}\right)_{+,1}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|e^{i\pi\pi(h_{1})}\xi-\zeta\right\|<\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\delta_{2,a}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\right),\quad\left\|e^{i\pi\pi(h_{2})}\eta-\zeta\right\|<\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\delta_{2,a}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\right),\end{split}

(4.56)

and self-adjoint
k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|e^{it\pi(k_{1})}-\mathbb{I}\right\|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8},\quad\left\|e^{it\pi(k_{2})}-\mathbb{I}\right\|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8},\quad t\in[0,1],\\
e^{i\pi(k_{1})}e^{i\pi\pi(h_{1})}\xi=\zeta,\quad e^{i\pi(k_{2})}e^{i\pi\pi(h_{2})}\eta=\zeta.\end{split}

(4.57)

Out of these
h_{1},h_{2},k_{1},k_{2}
,
as in the proof of Lemma 4.12 of
[
O1
]
, we can obtain the desired
v(t)
.

\square

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
{\mathcal{F}}
a finite subset
of
\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)_{1}
.
Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{i},\pi_{i})
,
i=0,1
be irreducible representations of

{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
such that
\pi_{i}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{i})
.
Let
\xi_{i}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
,
i=0,1
be unit vectors
satisfying

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\xi_{0},\pi_{0}(xy^{*})\xi_{0}\right\rangle-\left\langle\xi_{1},\pi_{1}(xy^{*})\xi_{1}\right\rangle\right|<\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\delta}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}),\quad x,y\in\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}}),

(4.58)

where
\tilde{\delta}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})
,
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{30}(\varepsilon,{\mathcal{F}})
are defined in Lemma
4.14
.
Then for any
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
, finite subset
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
,
there exists a norm-continuous path
v:[0,1]\to{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}^{(0)})

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}v(0)=\mathbb{I},\\
\left|\left\langle\xi_{0},\pi_{0}(a)\xi_{0}\right\rangle-\left\langle\xi_{1},\pi_{1}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v(1)\right)(a)\xi_{1}\right\rangle\right|<\varepsilon^{\prime},\quad a\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},\\
\left\|\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v(t)\right)(y)-y\right\|<\varepsilon,\quad y\in{\mathcal{F}},t\in[0,1].\end{split}

(4.59)

The proof of this Lemma is the same as that of
Lemma
4.10
, using Lemma
4.12
and Lemma
4.14
.

\square

Having Lemma
4.12
and Lemma
4.15
, the proof of Proposition
4.11

is the same as that of Proposition 4.2 of
[
O1
]
.

\square

Now we are ready to prove the main proposition of this section, Proposition
4.5
.

Let
\Theta_{\sigma}:=\Theta_{\Lambda_{\sigma}}
be the grading operator on

{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\sigma}}={\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{\sigma},{\mathfrak{C}}_{\sigma}\right)
for
\sigma=L,R
.

Case (a)

In this case,
\omega_{i}
,
i=0,1
has a GNS representation
of the form

({\mathcal{H}}_{L,i}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{R,i},\pi_{L,i}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R,i},\Omega_{i})

with irreducible covariant representations

({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma,i},\pi_{\sigma,i},\Gamma_{\sigma,i})
of
\Sigma_{\sigma,i}:=({\mathbb{Z}}_{2},{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\sigma}},\Theta_{\Lambda_{\sigma}})
,
\sigma=L,R
.
Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma,i,\pm},\pi_{\sigma,i,\pm})
be the decomposition associated to

({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma,i},\pi_{\sigma,i},\Gamma_{\sigma,i})
.
Choose and fix unit vectors
\xi_{\sigma,i}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma,i,+}
,
i=0,1
,
\sigma=L,R
,
and set
\tilde{\xi}_{\sigma,i}:=\xi_{\sigma,i}\oplus 0\in{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma,i,+}\oplus{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma,i,-}={\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma,i}
.
Then

\psi_{\sigma,i}:=\left\langle\tilde{\xi}_{\sigma,i},\pi_{\sigma,i}(\cdot)\tilde{\xi}_{\sigma,i}\right\rangle
,
i=0,1

defines a pure homogeneous state on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\sigma}}
,
\sigma=L,R

such that

\displaystyle\omega_{i}\simeq\left(\psi_{L,i}\hat{\otimes}\psi_{R,i}\right),\quad i=0,1.

(4.60)

By Proposition
4.6
,
there exist

\eta_{\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\sigma}}\right)

such that
\psi_{\sigma 1}=\psi_{\sigma 0}\circ\eta_{\sigma}
,
\sigma=L,R
.
From this we have

\omega_{1}\simeq\omega_{0}\circ\left(\eta_{L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{R}\right)
.

Case (b)

In this case

\omega_{i}
,
i=0,1
has a GNS representation
of the form

({\mathcal{H}}_{L,i}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{R,i}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{2},\pi_{i},\Omega_{i})
.
There are irreducible representations
\pi_{\sigma,i}

of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\sigma}}^{(0)}
, on
{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma,i}
,
\sigma=L,R
,
i=0,1

such that

\displaystyle\pi_{i}\left(a\hat{\otimes}b\right)=\pi_{L,i}(a)\otimes\pi_{R,i}(b)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2}},\quad a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{L}}^{(0)},\quad b\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{R}}^{(0)}.

(4.61)

We have

\displaystyle\pi_{i}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{L}}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L,i})\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{R,i}}\otimes\left({\mathbb{C}}\sigma_{z}+{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}\right),\quad\pi_{i}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{R}}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L,i}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{R,i})\otimes\left({\mathbb{C}}\sigma_{x}+{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}\right).

(4.62)

From this form, there are representations

\rho_{i}^{\sigma}
of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\sigma}}

on
{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma i}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{2}

such that

\displaystyle\pi_{i}(a)=\rho_{i}^{L}(a)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{Ri}},\quad a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{L}},\quad\pi_{i}(b)=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{Li}}\otimes\rho_{i}^{R}(b),\quad b\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{R}}.

(4.63)

Note that
\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{Li}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{Ri}}\otimes r_{\pm}^{L}
,

\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{Li}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{Ri}}\otimes r_{\pm}^{R}

with
r_{\pm}^{L}:=\frac{\mathbb{I}\pm\sigma_{z}}{2}
,
r_{\pm}^{R}:=\frac{\mathbb{I}\pm\sigma_{x}}{2}

commute with

\pi_{i}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{L}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

\pi_{i}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{R}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
, respectively.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\rho_{i\pm}^{L}(a)\otimes r_{\pm}^{L}:=\rho_{i}^{L}(a)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{Li}}\otimes r_{\pm}^{L}\right),\quad a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{L}},\quad\rho_{i\pm}^{R}(b)\otimes r_{\pm}^{R}:=\rho_{i}^{R}(b)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{Ri}}\otimes r_{\pm}^{R}\right),\quad b\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{R}}

(4.64)

define irreducible representations
\rho_{i\pm}^{\sigma}
of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\sigma}}

on
{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma i}
, for
i=0,1
,
\sigma=L,R
.
Note also that

\displaystyle\rho_{i\pm}^{\sigma}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\sigma}}^{(0)}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{B}}\left({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma i}\right).

(4.65)

Because
\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{Li}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{Ri}}\otimes\sigma_{y}
flips

\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{Li}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{Ri}}\otimes r_{\pm}^{\sigma}
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\rho_{i\pm}^{L}\circ\Theta_{L}(a)=\rho_{i\mp}^{L}(a),\quad a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{L}},\\
\rho_{i\pm}^{R}\circ\Theta_{R}(b)=\rho_{i\mp}^{R}(b),\quad b\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{R}}.\end{split}

(4.66)

Choose unit vectors
\xi_{\sigma i}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma i}
and define pure states

\displaystyle\psi_{\sigma i}:=\left\langle\xi_{\sigma i},\rho_{i+}^{\sigma}\left(\cdot\right)\xi_{\sigma i}\right\rangle,\quad i=0,1,\quad\sigma=L,R.

(4.67)

Hence applying Lemma
4.11
, there exist
automorphisms
\eta_{\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\sigma}}\right)

such that
\psi_{\sigma 1}=\psi_{\sigma 0}\circ\eta_{\sigma}
.
Then both of
({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma 1},\rho_{1+}^{\sigma},\xi_{\sigma 1})

and
({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma 0},\rho_{0+}^{\sigma}\circ\eta_{\sigma},\xi_{\sigma 0})

are GNS triple of
\psi_{1\sigma}
.
Therefore, there are unitaries
W_{\sigma}:{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma 0}\to{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma 1}

such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(W_{\sigma})\circ\rho_{0+}^{\sigma}\circ\eta_{\sigma}=\rho_{1+}^{\sigma}
,
\sigma=L,R
.
Combining this and (
4.66
),
we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(W_{\sigma})\circ\rho_{0-}^{\sigma}\circ\eta_{\sigma}=\rho_{1-}^{\sigma}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{L}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\left(\rho_{0}^{L}\circ\eta_{L}(a)\right)=\rho_{1}^{L}(a),\quad a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{L}},\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{R}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\left(\rho_{0}^{R}\circ\eta_{R}(b)\right)=\rho_{1}^{R}(b),\quad b\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{R}}.\end{split}

(4.68)

We claim
\pi_{1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{L}\otimes W_{R}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\left(\eta_{L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{R}\right)
.
In fact we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{L}\otimes W_{R}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\left(\eta_{L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{R}\right)(a)=\rho_{1}^{L}(a)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{R1}}=\pi_{1}(a),\quad a\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{L}},\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{L}\otimes W_{R}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\left(\eta_{L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{R}\right)(b)=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L1}}\otimes\rho_{1}^{R}(b)=\pi_{1}(b),\quad b\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{R}}.\end{split}

(4.69)

Hence
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{0}\left(\eta_{L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{R}\right)
are unitarily equivalent, and

\omega_{1}\simeq\omega_{0}\circ\left(\eta_{L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{R}\right)
.

Suppose that none of (a), (b) occurs.
In this case, one of

\pi_{\omega_{1}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{R}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
,

\pi_{\omega_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{R}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

is a factor and the other is not.
Therefore,
\omega_{1}
and
\omega_{0}
are not quasi-equivalent.

\square

We conclude this section with examples of the dichotomy of Lemma
4.4
.
It is clear that
\omega^{(0)}
satisfies (i) of Lemma
4.4
.

Recall the basis projection
p
(
1.10
) we considered in Section
1
, defining
\omega^{(0)}
.
Because
v_{\tau}
commutes with
{\mathfrak{C}}
,

q:=v_{\tau}^{*}pv_{\tau}

is also a basis projection of
({\mathfrak{h}},{\mathfrak{C}})
.
We denote by
\omega^{(1)}
the Fock state on
{\mathcal{A}}

given by this basis projection
q=v_{\tau}^{*}pv_{\tau}
.
Note that
\omega^{(1)}=\omega^{(0)}\circ\tau
,
and
\omega^{(1)}\circ\tau=\omega^{(0)}
.
To describe
v_{\tau}^{*}pv_{\tau}
,
set for each
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}

two dimensional space
{\mathcal{K}}_{(x,0)}

spanned by
\delta_{(2x-1,0)}
,
\delta_{(2x,0)}
.
Let
q_{x}
be an orthogonal projection on

{\mathcal{K}}_{(x,0)}
onto the
one-dimensional space spanned by

\delta_{(2x-1,0)}+i\delta_{(2x,0)}
.
By the definition, we have

\displaystyle q=v_{\tau}^{*}pv_{\tau}=Q^{(1)}_{L}\oplus Q^{(1)}_{R}\oplus q_{0},

(4.70)

with

\displaystyle Q^{(1)}_{L}:=p_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq-1}\times\left({\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\{0\}\right)}\oplus\bigoplus_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq-1}}q_{x},\quad Q^{(1)}_{R}:=p_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\times\left({\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\{0\}\right)}\oplus\bigoplus_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}}q_{x},

(4.71)

which are projections on

l^{2}\left({\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq-1}\times\left({\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\{0\}\right)\right)\oplus l^{2}\left({\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq-2}\times\{0\}\right)\subset{\mathfrak{h}}_{H_{L}}
and

l^{2}\left({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\times\left({\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\{0\}\right)\right)\oplus l^{2}\left({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}\times\{0\}\right)\subset{\mathfrak{h}}_{H_{R}}

respectively.
Noting that
q_{0}
is a projection on
a finite dimensional Hilbert space
{\mathcal{K}}_{(0,0)}
,
we see that
there are homogeneous states
\varphi_{L}
,

\varphi_{R}
on
{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}}

such that
\omega^{(1)}

is quasi-equivalent to
\varphi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\varphi_{R}
.
Hence
\omega^{(1)}
satisfies the split property for
H_{L}-H_{R}
-cut.
Now we would like to show (ii) of Lemma
4.4
occurs.
With a unitary
\vartheta:=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathfrak{h}}_{H_{L}}}\oplus-\mathbb{I}_{{\mathfrak{h}}_{H_{R}}}
,
we note that
\omega^{(1)}\circ\Theta_{R}

is a Fock state on
{\mathcal{A}}
given by the basis projection

\vartheta q\vartheta^{*}
.
(Note that
\vartheta q\vartheta^{*}

is a basis projection because
\vartheta
and
{\mathfrak{C}}

commute.)
From the definition, this projection
\vartheta q\vartheta^{*}

can be decomposed as

\displaystyle\vartheta q\vartheta^{*}=Q^{(1)}_{L}\oplus Q^{(1)}_{R}\oplus\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{(0,0)}}-q_{0}\right).

(4.72)

Comparing this and (
4.70
),

\displaystyle q\wedge(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathfrak{h}}}-\vartheta q\vartheta^{*})=q_{0}.

(4.73)

Noting
q_{0}
is a one-rank projection, from Theorem 6.30 of
[
EK
]
,

\omega^{(1)}|_{{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}
and
\omega^{(1)}|_{{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\Theta_{R}

are not equivalent.
Hence (ii) of Lemma
4.4

holds for
\omega^{(1)}
.

Combining this with Proposition
4.5
, we conclude that
any homogeneous pure state satisfying the split property with respect to
the
H_{L}-H_{R}
-cut can be obtained out of either

\omega^{(0)}
or
\omega^{(1)}
via an automorphism of the form

\eta_{L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{R}
.

5
The
\mathcal{PD}_{0}(G)
-valued index

Now we derive the
\mathcal{PD}_{0}(G)
-valued index out of SPT-phases.

5.1
An Overview

A brief description of the derivation is as follows.
From Theorem
3.1
, the ground states in SPT-phases are of the form

\omega=\omega^{(0)}\circ\alpha

with
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.
Using the factorization property (
3.9
),
we can show that the difference between
\omega^{(0)}\alpha\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha^{-1}

and
\omega^{(0)}
is localized around
x
-axis, and in particular,

\omega^{(0)}\alpha\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha^{-1}
satisfies the split property.
Recall that then there is a dichotomy given by Lemma
4.4

for this
\omega^{(0)}\alpha\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha^{-1}
.
Let us set
a_{\omega}(g)=0
if (i) of Lemma
4.4
occurs
and
a_{\omega}(g)=1
if (ii) of Lemma
4.4
occurs.
Applying the result Proposition
4.5
from the previous section,
it allows us to show

\omega\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\simeq\omega\circ\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\left(\eta_{g,L}^{\epsilon}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}^{\epsilon}\right)
for
\epsilon=\pm
, with
\eta_{g,\sigma}^{\epsilon}
automorphism localized in
C_{\theta}\cap H_{\sigma}
,

\sigma=L,R
. (Proposition
5.2
, Remark
5.4
.)

Next we decompose
\alpha
as (
3.8
) where
\alpha_{L}
,
\alpha_{R}
are automorphisms localized to the left and right infinite planes
H_{L}
,
H_{R}

while
\Upsilon
is localized in
\left(C_{\theta}\right)^{c}
.
We then have
\omega\simeq\left(\omega_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Upsilon

with pure states
\omega_{L}
,
\omega_{R}
on the left and right infinite planes.
Hence we obtain

\left(\omega_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Upsilon\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\simeq\left(\omega_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Upsilon\circ\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\left(\eta_{g,L}^{\epsilon}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}^{\epsilon}\right)
.

Setting

\gamma_{g}^{\epsilon}:=\beta_{g}^{U}\left(\eta_{g,L}^{\epsilon}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}^{\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\tau^{-a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}

for
\epsilon=\pm
,
g\in G
,
we have
\left(\omega_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Upsilon\gamma_{g}^{\epsilon}\simeq\left(\omega_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Upsilon
.
Repeated use of this gives us

\left(\omega_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Upsilon\gamma_{g}^{\epsilon}\gamma_{h}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}{\gamma_{gh}^{\epsilon}}^{-1}\simeq\left(\omega_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Upsilon.

But one can see that

\gamma_{g}^{\epsilon}\gamma_{h}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}{\gamma_{gh}^{\epsilon}}^{-1}=\widehat{\bigotimes}_{\sigma=L,R}\zeta_{g,h,\sigma}^{\epsilon}

with
\zeta_{g,h,\sigma}^{\epsilon}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}\cap H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}}}\right)

(
5.35
),
and it commutes with
\Upsilon
.
Hence we obtain
\omega_{L}\alpha_{L}\zeta_{g,h,L}^{\epsilon}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{R}\alpha_{R}\zeta_{g,h,R}^{\epsilon}\simeq\omega_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{R}\alpha_{R}
, which implies

\omega_{\sigma}\alpha_{\sigma}\zeta_{g,h,\sigma}^{\epsilon}\simeq\omega_{\sigma}\alpha_{\sigma}
.
This equivalence gives us a unitary
u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)
in
Lemma
5.6
, implementing
\zeta_{g,h,\sigma}^{\epsilon}

in the GNS-representation of
\omega_{\sigma}\alpha_{\sigma}
.
Note also that
\left(\omega_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Upsilon\gamma_{g}^{\epsilon}\simeq\left(\omega_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Upsilon

means there is a unitary
W_{g}^{\epsilon}
in the GNS-representation of

\omega_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{R}\alpha_{R}

implementing
\Upsilon\gamma_{g}^{\epsilon}\Upsilon^{-1}
.
The unitaries
W_{g}^{\epsilon}
and
u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)

are homogeneous with respect to the grading
and we denote their grade by
b^{\epsilon}_{g}
,
\kappa_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)
.
Using the associativity of automorphisms, it turns out that

W_{g}^{\epsilon}
and
u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)

satisfy some non-trivial relation (
5.47
) Lemma
5.10
, with some
phase factor
c^{\epsilon}(g,h,k)\in{\mathrm{U}}(1)
.
Considering the grading of this equation, we obtain some relation between

b^{\epsilon}_{g}
,
\kappa_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)
.
Juggling the relation (
5.47
), we obtain
relation between
\kappa_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)
and
c^{\epsilon}(g,h,k)\in{\mathrm{U}}(1)
.
This gives an element of
\mathcal{PD}_{0}(G)
.

The difference of the choice of objects we introduced to define the index
can be implemented by some unitary.
Writing such relation down brings
us to the equivalence relation we introduced
to define
\mathcal{PD}_{0}(G)
.

As we saw in Lemma
3.2
, our translation
\tau
shifts supports of automorphism
which is sometimes inconvenient. However, Proposition
7.1

proved in Section
7
allow us to
modify them suitably. We occasionally use this Proposition
7.1

in this section.

5.2
Effective excitation caused by
\beta_{g}^{U}

In this subsection, we investigate the effective excitation caused by
\beta_{g}^{U}

on our SPT-ground state
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
.
We denote by
\left({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma},\pi_{\sigma},\Omega_{\sigma}\right)

a GNS triple of
\omega_{\sigma}

for
\sigma=L,R
.
Because
\omega_{\sigma}
is homogeneous,
there is a self-adjoint unitary
\Gamma_{\sigma}
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}

such that
\Gamma_{\sigma}\pi_{\sigma}(A)\Omega_{\sigma}=\pi_{\sigma}\left(\Theta_{\sigma}(A)\right)\Omega_{\sigma}
for all
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}}
.
From (
1.13
),
({\mathcal{H}}_{L}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{R},\pi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R},\Omega_{L}\otimes\Omega_{R})

is a GNS representation of
\omega^{(0)}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

For
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits

\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
,

0<\theta<\theta^{\prime}<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
(\alpha_{U},\alpha_{D},\Xi_{L},\Xi_{R})\in{\mathcal{D}}^{H}(\alpha,\theta)
and
g\in G
,
there are homogeneous states

\varphi_{L}

\varphi_{R}

on
{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}\cap H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}
,

{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}\cap H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}

such that

\displaystyle\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\sim_{\mathop{q.e.}}\varphi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\varphi_{R}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}^{c}}},

(5.1)

\displaystyle\left.\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}}\sim_{\mathop{q.e.}}\varphi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\varphi_{R},

(5.2)

\displaystyle\left.\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}^{c}}}\sim_{\mathop{q.e.}}\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}^{c}}}.

(5.3)

Set
c_{U}:=1
and
c_{D}:=-1
.
Because
\alpha_{\zeta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\zeta}^{c_{\zeta}}})

for
\zeta=U,D

we can decompose
\alpha_{\zeta}^{-1}
as

\displaystyle\alpha_{\zeta}^{-1}=\left(\alpha_{C_{\theta},L,\zeta}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{C_{\theta},R,\zeta}\right)\circ\alpha_{C_{\theta}^{c},\zeta}\circ\left(\alpha_{\partial C_{\theta},L,\zeta}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{\partial C_{\theta},R,\zeta}\right)\circ({\rm{inner}}),

(5.4)

where

\displaystyle\alpha_{C_{\theta},\sigma,\zeta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}\cap H_{\sigma}\cap H_{\zeta}}\right),\quad\alpha_{C_{\theta}^{c},\zeta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}^{c}\cap H_{\zeta}}\right),\quad\alpha_{\partial C_{\theta},\sigma,\zeta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta^{\prime}}\cap H_{\sigma}\cap H_{\zeta}}\right),

(5.5)

for
\sigma=L,R
,
\zeta=U,D
.
Using the decomposition (
5.4
)
and the support of the automorphisms there in (
5.5
),
we rewrite
\alpha\beta_{g}\alpha^{-1}

as

\displaystyle\alpha\beta_{g}\alpha^{-1}=\left(\alpha_{U}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{D}\right)\circ\beta_{g}\left(\alpha_{U}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{D}\right)^{-1}\left(Y_{L}\hat{\otimes}Y_{R}\right)\circ({\rm{inner}})

(5.6)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}Y_{\sigma}:=&\left(\alpha_{\partial C_{\theta},\sigma,U}^{-1}\alpha_{C_{\theta},\sigma,U}^{-1}\right)\hat{\otimes}\left(\alpha_{\partial C_{\theta},\sigma,D}^{-1}\alpha_{C_{\theta},\sigma,D}^{-1}\right)\left(\beta_{g}^{H_{\sigma}}\right)^{-1}\Xi_{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{H_{\sigma}}\Xi_{\sigma}^{-1}\left(\alpha_{C_{\theta},\sigma,U}\alpha_{\partial C_{\theta},\sigma,U}\right)\hat{\otimes}\left(\alpha_{C_{\theta},\sigma,D}\alpha_{\partial C_{\theta},\sigma,D}\right)\\
&\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta^{\prime}}\cap H_{\sigma}}\right),\quad\sigma=L,R.\end{split}

(5.7)

Substituting this, from the
\beta_{g}
-invariance of
\omega
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega^{(0)}=\omega^{(0)}\alpha\beta_{g}\alpha^{-1}=\omega^{(0)}\left(\alpha_{U}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{D}\right)\circ\beta_{g}\left(\alpha_{U}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{D}\right)^{-1}\left(Y_{L}\hat{\otimes}Y_{R}\right)\circ({\rm{inner}}).\end{split}

(5.8)

From this, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\right)\hat{\otimes}\left(\omega_{p_{H_{D}}}\alpha_{D}\beta_{g}^{D}\alpha_{D}^{-1}\right)\simeq\left(\omega_{p_{H_{L}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}}{Y_{L}^{\prime}}^{-1}\right)\hat{\otimes}\left(\omega_{p_{H_{R}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}}{Y_{R}^{\prime}}^{-1}\right)\hat{\otimes}\omega_{p_{C_{\theta^{\prime}}^{c}}}.\end{split}

(5.9)

Here,
Y^{\prime}_{\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}\right)

are automorphisms
such that

\displaystyle{Y_{L}^{\prime}}^{-1}\hat{\otimes}{Y_{R}^{\prime}}^{-1}=\left(Y_{L}\hat{\otimes}Y_{R}\right)^{-1}\left(\alpha_{U}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{D}\right)\circ\left(\beta_{g}^{{\mathbb{Z}}\times\{0\}}\right)^{-1}\left(\alpha_{U}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{D}\right)^{-1}({\rm{inner}})

(5.10)

Note that

\varphi_{L}:=\omega_{p_{H_{L}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}}{Y_{L}^{\prime}}^{-1}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap H_{L}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}}

\varphi_{R}:=\omega_{p_{H_{R}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}}{Y_{R}^{\prime}}^{-1}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap H_{R}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}}

are homogeneous states on
{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}\cap H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}
,

{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}\cap H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}
respectively.

Note that both sides of (
5.9
)
are pure and homogeneous state on
{\mathcal{A}}
.
Restricting (
5.9
) to
{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}}
,
from Lemma
4.1

we obtain (
5.1
).
If we restrict (
5.9
) to
{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}}
,
then we have
(
5.2
)
from Lemma
4.1
.
If we restrict (
5.9
) to
{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime}}^{c}}
,
then we have
(
5.3
)
from Lemma
4.1
.

\square

The following Proposition gives the effective excitation caused by
\beta_{g}^{U}
on
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
.
Then there is a group homomorpshims

a_{\omega}:G\to\{0,1\}={\mathbb{Z}}_{2}

satisfying the following.

(i)

For any
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
, and
g\in G

there are

\eta_{g,L}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}^{c_{L}}\cap C_{\theta}}\right)
,

\eta_{g,R}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}^{c_{R}}\cap C_{\theta}}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\omega\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\simeq\omega\circ\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)}\left(\eta_{g,L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}\right).

(5.11)

(ii)

If there is
g\in G
,
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,

a^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
and

\eta_{g,L}^{\prime}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}^{c_{L}}\cap C_{\theta}}\right)
,

\eta_{g,R}^{\prime}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}^{c_{R}}\cap C_{\theta}}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\omega\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\simeq\omega\circ\tau^{a^{\prime}}\left(\eta_{g,L}^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}^{\prime}\right),

(5.12)

then
a^{\prime}=a_{\omega}(g)
.

Definition 5.3
.

Proposition
5.2

defines
a
*
-homomorphism

a_{\omega}:G\to\{0,1\}={\mathbb{Z}}_{2}

for each
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
.
We will use this symbol henceforth.

Existence
:
Fix any
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
. We show that for each

g\in G
,
there are
a_{\omega}(g)=0,1
,

\eta_{g,L}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}^{c_{L}}\cap C_{\theta}}\right)
,

\eta_{g,R}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}^{c_{R}}\cap C_{\theta}}\right)

satisfying (
5.11
).
Choose some
0<\theta^{\prime}<\theta^{\prime\prime}<\theta
,

\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,
and
(\alpha_{U},\alpha_{D},\Xi_{L},\Xi_{R})\in{\mathcal{D}}^{H}(\alpha,\theta^{\prime})
.
Then by Lemma
5.1
,
there are homogeneous states
\varphi_{L}
,
\varphi_{R}

on

{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap H_{L}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}
,

{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap H_{R}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}

such that

\displaystyle\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\sim_{\mathop{q.e.}}\varphi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\varphi_{R}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}},

(5.13)

\displaystyle\left.\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}}\sim_{\mathop{q.e.}}\varphi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\varphi_{R},

(5.14)

\displaystyle\left.\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}}\sim_{\mathop{q.e.}}\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}}.

(5.15)

Note that

{\mathfrak{h}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}
,

{\mathfrak{h}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}
,

\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}

satisfy the conditions of

{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}
,
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
,
\omega

in Lemma
4.4

respectively.
Applying Lemma
4.4
,
either (i) or (ii) of Lemma
4.4
occurs.
From the quasi-equivalence (
5.13
),
the von Neumann algebras

\pi_{\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

and
\pi_{\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

are
*
-isomorphic.
As
\pi_{\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
is a type
I
factor,
(because
\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}}
is a pure state),
it means

\pi_{\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

is also a type
I
factor.
It means from Lemma
4.4
that Lemma
4.4
(i)
occurs.
Hence for both of pure homogeneous states

\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}

and
\omega^{(0)}
,
(i) of Lemma
4.4
occurs, and we may apply
Proposition
4.5
.
Applying Lemma
4.5
,
there are automorphisms
S\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}\right)
,
T\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\simeq\left(\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}S\right)\hat{\otimes}\left(\omega_{p_{H_{U}}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}T\right).

(5.16)

Note that both sides are pure homogeneous states.
Therefore, applying Lemma
4.2
, and (
5.15
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}}\sim_{\mathop{q.e.}}\left.\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}}\sim_{\mathop{q.e.}}\omega_{p_{H_{U}}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}T.

(5.17)

Because both of
\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}}
and
\omega_{p_{H_{U}}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}T

are pure, we conclude that they are equvalent.
Substituting this to (
5.16
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\simeq\left(\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}S\right)\hat{\otimes}\left(\omega_{p_{H_{U}}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}T\right)\simeq\left(\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}S\right)\hat{\otimes}\left(\omega_{p_{H_{U}}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{c}}\right).

(5.18)

Because both sides of this equation are homogeneous and
pure, applying Lemma
4.1
, and combining it with
(
5.14
)
we obtain

\displaystyle\varphi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\varphi_{R}\sim_{\mathop{q.e.}}\left.\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\right|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}}\sim_{\mathop{q.e.}}\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}S.

(5.19)

Hence

{\mathfrak{h}}_{H_{U}\cap H_{L}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}
,

{\mathfrak{h}}_{H_{U}\cap H_{R}\cap C_{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}
,

\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}S
satisfies
the condition of
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}
,
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
,

\omega
in Lemma
4.4
holds and
(i) or (ii) of Lemma
4.4
holds for
\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}S
.

If

\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}S
satisfies (i) of Lemma
4.4
,
we set
a_{\omega}(g):=0
, and
if
\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}S
satisfies (ii) of Lemma
4.4
,
we set
a_{\omega}(g):=1
.

If
a_{\omega}(g)=0
, because
\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}
also satisfies
(i) of Lemma
4.4
,
from Proposition
4.5
, there are

\tilde{\eta}_{g,L}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap H_{L}^{c_{L}}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}\right)
,

\tilde{\eta}_{g,R}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap H_{R}^{c_{R}}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}S\simeq\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}\circ\left(\tilde{\eta}_{g,L}\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\eta}_{g,R}\right).

(5.20)

Here, we used Proposition
7.1
in section
7

to take the support of
\tilde{\eta}_{g,\sigma}
from

H_{U}\cap H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}
, not just

H_{U}\cap H_{\sigma}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}
.

If
a_{\omega}(g)=1
, because
\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}\tau
also satisfies
(ii) of Lemma
4.4
,
from the same argument as the last paragraph of Section
4
, there are

\tilde{\eta}_{g,L}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap H_{L}^{c_{L}}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}\right)
,

\tilde{\eta}_{g,R}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{U}\cap H_{R}^{c_{R}}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}S\simeq\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}\circ\tau\circ\left(\tilde{\eta}_{g,L}\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\eta}_{g,R}\right).

(5.21)

(Again we used Proposition
4.5
and
Proposition
7.1
.)

Combining these with (
5.18
), we obtain

\displaystyle\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\simeq\omega_{p_{H_{U}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}\circ\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)}\circ\left(\tilde{\eta}_{g,L}\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\eta}_{g,R}\right)\hat{\otimes}\left(\omega_{p_{H_{U}}\cap C_{{{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}}^{c}}\right)=\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\circ\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)}\circ\left(\tilde{\eta}_{g,L}\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\eta}_{g,R}\right).

(5.22)

As in the proof of Lemma
5.1
(
5.6
),
there are
Z_{\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}\cap C_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}}\right)
,

\sigma=L,R

such that

\displaystyle\alpha\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha^{-1}=\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\circ\left(Z_{L}\hat{\otimes}Z_{R}\right)\circ({\rm{inner}}).

(5.23)

From this and (
5.22
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\omega^{(0)}\alpha\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha^{-1}\simeq\left(\omega_{p_{H_{D}}}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{p_{H_{U}}}\alpha_{U}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{U}^{-1}\right)\circ\left(Z_{L}\hat{\otimes}Z_{R}\right)\simeq\omega^{(0)}\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)}\left(\tilde{\eta}_{g,L}Z_{L}\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\eta}_{g,R}Z_{R}\right)\\
&=\omega^{(0)}\alpha\circ\alpha^{-1}\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)}\left(\tilde{\eta}_{g,L}Z_{L}\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\eta}_{g,R}Z_{R}\right)\alpha\circ\alpha^{-1}.\end{split}

(5.24)

Note from Lemma
3.2

that there are automorphisms

\eta_{g,L}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}\cap H_{L}}\right)
,

\eta_{g,R}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}\cap H_{R}}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\alpha^{-1}\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)}\left(\tilde{\eta}_{g,L}Z_{L}\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\eta}_{g,R}Z_{R}\right)\alpha=\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)}\left(\eta_{g,L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}\right)\circ({\rm{inner}}).

(5.25)

From Lemma
7.1
, we may assume that

\eta_{g,L}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}^{c_{L}}\cap C_{\theta}}\right)
,

\eta_{g,R}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}^{c_{R}}\cap C_{\theta}}\right)
.
Substituting this to (
5.24
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega\beta_{g}^{U}=\omega^{(0)}\alpha\beta_{g}^{U}\simeq\omega^{(0)}\alpha\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)}\left(\eta_{g,L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}\right)=\omega\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)}\left(\eta_{g,L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}\right).\end{split}

(5.26)

Uniqueness :

Suppose for
i=1,2
, there are

0<\theta_{i}<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
a_{1}(g),a_{2}(g)=0,1
,

\eta_{g,L}^{(i)}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}^{c_{L}}\cap C_{\theta_{i}}}\right)
,

\eta_{g,R}^{(i)}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}^{c_{R}}\cap C_{\theta_{i}}}\right)

satisfying

\displaystyle\omega\beta_{g}^{U}\simeq\omega\tau^{a_{1}}\left(\eta_{g,L}^{(1)}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}^{(1)}\right)\simeq\omega\tau^{a_{2}}\left(\eta_{g,L}^{(2)}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}^{(2)}\right).

(5.27)

We set
\theta:=\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}
.
Let
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
and
\theta<\theta^{\prime}<\frac{\pi}{2}
.
Then from (
5.27
), Lemma
3.2

there are some automorphisms
\xi_{\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta^{\prime}}\cap H_{\sigma}}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega^{(0)}\simeq\omega^{(0)}\circ\alpha\tau^{a_{1}}\left(\eta_{g,L}^{(1)}\left(\eta_{g,L}^{(2)}\right)^{-1}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}^{(1)}\left(\eta_{g,R}^{(2)}\right)^{-1}\right)\tau^{-a_{2}}\alpha^{-1}\simeq\omega^{(0)}\tau^{a_{1}-a_{2}}\left(\xi_{L}\otimes\xi_{R}\right).\end{split}

(5.28)

By Lemma
4.4
, if
a_{1}-a_{2}=-1,1
, then

\omega^{(0)}\tau^{a_{1}-a_{2}}\left(\xi_{L}\otimes\xi_{R}\right)

satisfies Lemma
4.4
(ii) and from Proposition
4.5
it
cannot be equivalent to
\omega^{(0)}
.
Therefore, from (
5.28
),

a_{1}=a_{2}
.

Group homomorphism:

Let
g,h\in G
and
\eta_{g,\sigma},\eta_{h,\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}\cap H_{\sigma}}\right)

satisfying (
5.11
)
for
g,h
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\omega\beta_{gh}^{U}=\omega\beta_{g}^{U}\beta_{h}^{U}\simeq\omega\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)}\left(\eta_{g,L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}\right)\beta_{h}^{U}\\
&=\omega\beta_{h}^{U}\left(\beta_{h}^{U}\right)^{-1}\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)}\left(\eta_{g,L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}\right)\beta_{h}^{U}\\
&\simeq\omega\tau^{a_{\omega}(h)}\left(\eta_{h,L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{h,R}\right)\left(\beta_{h}^{U}\right)^{-1}\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)}\left(\eta_{g,L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}\right)\beta_{h}^{U}\\
&\simeq\omega\tau^{a_{\omega}(h)+a_{\omega}(g)}\left(\eta_{gh,L}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{gh,R}\right),\end{split}

(5.29)

with some

\eta_{gh,\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}}\cap C_{\theta}})
,
using Proposition
7.1
.
If
a_{\omega}(h)+a_{\omega}(g)=0,1
, then
from the uniqueness, we have
a_{\omega}(gh)=a_{\omega}(g)+a_{\omega}(h)
.
If
a_{\omega}(h)+a_{\omega}(g)=2
, then combining with Lemma
3.4
,
we have

\omega\beta_{gh}^{U}\simeq\omega\left(\tilde{\eta}_{gh,L}\hat{\otimes}\tilde{\eta}_{gh,R}\right),

with some

\tilde{\eta}_{gh,\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}}\cap C_{\theta}})

and we get
a_{\omega}(gh)=0
.
Hence identifying
\{0,1\}={\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
, we see that

a_{\omega}:G\to\{0,1\}={\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
is a group homomorphism.

\square

Remark 5.4
.

If
a_{\omega}(g)=1
, then
from Lemma
3.4
,
for any
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}

we have

\displaystyle\omega\beta_{g}^{U}\simeq\omega\tau^{-1}\left(\eta_{g,L}^{(-1)}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}^{(-1)}\right),

(5.30)

for some
\eta_{g,\sigma}^{(-1)}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}}\cap C_{\theta}})
.

Hence for each
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
, the following set is not empty.

Definition 5.5
.

For each
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
and
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
we set

\displaystyle I(\omega,\theta):=\left\{\left(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}\right)_{g\in G,\sigma=L,R,\epsilon=\pm}\middle|\begin{gathered}\eta_{g,L}^{\epsilon}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}^{c_{L}}\cap C_{\theta}}\right),\quad\eta_{g,R}^{\epsilon}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}^{c_{R}}\cap C_{\theta}}\right)\\
\omega\beta_{g}^{U}\simeq\omega\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\left(\eta_{g,L}^{\epsilon}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{g,R}^{\epsilon}\right)\end{gathered}\right\}.

For
\left(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}\right)\in I(\omega,\theta)
,
we set
\eta_{g}^{\epsilon}:=\eta_{gL}^{\epsilon}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon}
.

5.3
Derivation of an element in
\mathcal{PD}(G)

Using the elements in
I(\omega,\theta)
, we derive some element
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{PD}}(G)
out of SPT-ground states.
We set

\displaystyle\begin{split}{c_{L}^{(1)}}:=-4=c_{L}+1,\quad{c_{R}^{(1)}}:=4=c_{R}-1,\quad{c_{L}^{(2)}}:=-3=c_{L}+2,\quad{c_{R}^{(2)}}:=3=c_{R}-2.\end{split}

(5.33)

The conjugation by
\tau
or
\tau^{-1}

maps an automorphism supported at
C_{\theta}\cap H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}}

to an automorphism supported at
C_{\theta}\cap H_{\sigma}^{{c_{\sigma}^{(1)}}}
.
The conjugation by
\tau^{2}
or
\tau^{-2}

maps an automorphism supported at
C_{\theta}\cap H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}}

to an automorphism supported at
C_{\theta}\cap H_{\sigma}^{{c_{\sigma}^{(2)}}}

(Lemma
3.2
).

Lemma 5.6
.

For any
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
,
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,

0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\in I(\omega,\theta)
,

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\in{\mathcal{D}}^{V}(\alpha,\theta)
,
there are unitaries

u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma})
,

g,h\in G
,
\sigma=L,R
,
\epsilon=\pm 1

such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\right)\pi_{\sigma}\alpha_{\sigma}=\pi_{\sigma}\alpha_{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{U\sigma}\left(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\beta_{h}^{U\sigma}\left(\left(\eta_{h\sigma}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)_{-a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\eta_{gh,\sigma}^{\epsilon}\left(\beta_{gh}^{U\sigma}\right)^{-1}.

(5.34)

Set

\displaystyle\zeta_{g,h,\sigma}^{\epsilon}:=\beta_{g}^{U\sigma}\left(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\beta_{h}^{U\sigma}\left(\left(\eta_{h\sigma}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)_{-a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\eta_{gh,\sigma}^{\epsilon}\left(\beta_{gh}^{U\sigma}\right)^{-1}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}\cap H_{\sigma}^{{c_{\sigma}^{(1)}}}}\right)

(5.35)

for
g,h\in G
,
\sigma=L,R
,
\epsilon=\pm 1
.
Note from
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\in I(\omega,\theta)

and

\displaystyle a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon+a_{\omega}(h)(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon=a_{\omega}(gh)\epsilon

(5.36)

that

\displaystyle\omega\circ\left(\zeta_{g,h,L}^{\epsilon}\hat{\otimes}\zeta_{g,h,R}^{\epsilon}\right)\simeq\omega.

(5.37)

Substituting
\omega\simeq\omega^{(0)}\left(\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}\right)\Upsilon
,
from the commutativity of
\Upsilon
and
\zeta_{g,h,\sigma}^{\epsilon}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\bigotimes}_{\sigma=L,R}\omega_{p_{H_{\sigma}}}\alpha_{\sigma}\zeta_{g,h,\sigma}^{\epsilon}\simeq\widehat{\bigotimes}_{\sigma=L,R}\omega_{p_{H_{\sigma}}}\alpha_{\sigma}.

(5.38)

Because both sides are pure homogeneous states,
Lemma
4.1
implies

\displaystyle\omega_{p_{H_{\sigma}}}\alpha_{\sigma}\zeta_{g,h,\sigma}^{\epsilon}\simeq\omega_{p_{H_{\sigma}}}\alpha_{\sigma}.

(5.39)

Hence we complete the proof.

\square

Lemma 5.7
.

For any
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
,
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,

0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\in I(\omega,\theta)
,

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\in{\mathcal{D}}^{V}(\alpha,\theta)
,
there are unitaries

W_{g}^{\epsilon}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{R})

such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)\circ\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)=\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Upsilon\beta_{g}^{U}\left(\eta_{gL}^{\epsilon}\hat{\otimes}\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\tau^{-a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\Upsilon^{-1}.

(5.40)

This is immediate from
\omega\simeq\omega^{(0)}\left(\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}\right)\Upsilon
,
and
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\in I(\omega,\theta)
.

\square

Definition 5.8
.

For any
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
,
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,

0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\in I(\omega,\theta)
,

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\in{\mathcal{D}}^{V}(\alpha,\theta)
,
we denote by
\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)

the set of
\left((W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)

satisfying (
5.40
) and (
5.34
).
From Lemma
C.1
, there are
b_{g}^{\epsilon},\kappa_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\in\{0,1\}
such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\Gamma_{L}\otimes\Gamma_{R}\right)\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)=(-1)^{b_{g}^{\epsilon}}W_{g}^{\epsilon},\quad\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\Gamma_{\sigma}\left(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\right)=(-1)^{\kappa_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)}u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h).

(5.41)

Because
\pi_{L},\pi_{R}
are irreducible,
b_{g}^{\epsilon},\kappa_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\in\{0,1\}
are independent of the choice of
\left((W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)
.
(The independence of them from the choice of
\left((W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)

is because the ambiguity of
\left((W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)
are just
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-phases.
)
Define

b\in C^{1}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})
,
\kappa_{\sigma}\in C^{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})
by

\displaystyle b(g):=\left(b_{g}^{+1},b_{g}^{-1}\right),\quad\kappa_{\sigma}(g,h):=\left(\kappa_{\sigma}^{+1}(g,h),\kappa_{\sigma}^{-1}(g,h)\right)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{2},\quad g,h\in G.

(5.42)

We denote
b\in C^{1}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})
,
\kappa_{\sigma}\in C^{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})

defined in (
5.42
)
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}b\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right),\kappa_{\sigma}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right),\end{split}

(5.43)

respectively.

In the following theorem, we used notation from Definition
5.8
.

Lemma 5.9
.

For any
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
,
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,

0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\in I(\omega,\theta)
,

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\in{\mathcal{D}}^{V}(\alpha,\theta)
,

\left((W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)
the following holds.

(i)

u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\in\left(\pi_{\sigma}\alpha_{\sigma}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\cap H_{\sigma}^{c^{(1)}_{\sigma}}\right)^{c}\cap H_{\sigma}}\right)\right)^{\prime}
.

(ii)

For any
x\in{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{R})
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}W_{h}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}{W_{gh}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\right)(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)}\right)(x).

(5.44)

(iii)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)\left({\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\left(\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\cap H_{R}^{c^{(2)}_{R}}\right)^{c}\cap H_{R}}\right)\right)^{\prime}\right)\subset{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{R})

(5.45)

(iv)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}W_{h}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}(k,f)\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u^{\epsilon}_{R}(g,h)\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)}\right)W_{gh}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}(k,f)\right)

(5.46)

The proof is the same as that of Lemma 2.3 of
[
O4
]
,
using Lemma
C.1
.
We just need to care about the fact that
\tau
can shift the support of automorphism
according to Lemma
3.3
.

\square

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
,
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,

0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\in I(\omega,\theta)
,

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\in{\mathcal{D}}^{V}(\alpha,\theta)
,

\left((W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)
.
Then there is some
c_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h,k)\in{\mathrm{U}}(1)
for each
g,h,k\in G
and
\epsilon=\pm 1
,
\sigma=L,R

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&W_{g}^{\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}(h,k)\right){W_{g}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,hk)\right)=c_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h,k)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)u_{R}^{\epsilon}(gh,k)\right)\\
&W_{g}^{\epsilon}\left(u_{L}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}(h,k)\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}(h,k)}\right){W_{g}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\left(u_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,hk)\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,hk)}\right)=c_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h,k)u_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)u_{L}^{\epsilon}(gh,k)\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)+\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(gh,k)}.\end{split}

(5.47)

Definition 5.11
.

We define

c\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon),(W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)\in C^{3}(G,{\mathrm{U}}(1)\oplus{\mathrm{U}}(1))

by a map
c:G^{\times 3}\to{\mathrm{U}}(1)\oplus{\mathrm{U}}(1)
such that

\displaystyle c(g,h,k):=\left(c_{R}^{+1}(g,h,k),c_{R}^{-1}(g,h,k)\right)\in{\mathrm{U}}(1)\oplus{\mathrm{U}}(1),\quad g,h,k\in G

(5.48)

with
c_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h,k)
in Lemma
5.10
.

The existence of
c_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h,k)\in{\mathrm{U}}(1)
satisfying first equation of (
5.47
) follows from

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)u_{R}^{\epsilon}(gh,k)\right)\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\beta_{ghk}^{UR}\left(\eta_{ghk,R}^{\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\tau^{-a_{\omega}(ghk)\epsilon}\\
&=\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\beta_{g}^{UR}\left(\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\tau^{-a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\beta_{h}^{UR}\left(\eta_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\tau^{-a_{\omega}(h)(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\beta_{k}^{UR}\left(\eta_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\tau^{-a_{\omega}(k)(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}(h,k)\right){W_{g}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,hk)\right)\right)\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\beta_{ghk}^{UR}\left(\eta_{ghk,R}^{\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\tau^{-a_{\omega}(ghk)\epsilon}.\end{split}

(5.49)

\square

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
,
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,

0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\in I(\omega,\theta)
,

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\in{\mathcal{D}}^{V}(\alpha,\theta)
,
and
\left((W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)
.
Recall
a_{\omega}\in H^{1}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&c\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon),(W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)=:c\in C^{3}(G,{\mathrm{U}}(1)\oplus{\mathrm{U}}(1))\\
&b\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)=:b\in C^{1}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}),\\
&\kappa_{\sigma}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)=:\kappa_{\sigma}\in C^{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}),\quad\sigma=L,R\end{split}

(5.50)

defined in Definition
5.3
and Definition
5.11
,
Definition
5.8
respectively.
Then we have

\displaystyle\left(c,\kappa_{R},\kappa_{L},b,a_{\omega}\right)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{PD}}(G).

(5.51)

If
\left(({W^{\prime}}_{g}^{\epsilon}),({u^{\prime}}_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)
is another choice
from
\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)

and
c\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon),({W^{\prime}}_{g}^{\epsilon}),({u^{\prime}}_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)=:c^{\prime}
,
then we have

\displaystyle\left(c,\kappa_{R},\kappa_{L},b,a_{\omega}\right)\sim_{\mathcal{PD}(G)}\left(c^{\prime},\kappa_{R},\kappa_{L},b,a_{\omega}\right).

(5.52)

Definition 5.13
.

From Lemma
5.12
,
for each
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
,
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,

0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\in I(\omega,\theta)
,

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\in{\mathcal{D}}^{V}(\alpha,\theta)
,
we may define

\displaystyle h^{(1)}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right):=\left[\left(c,\kappa_{R},\kappa_{L},b,a_{\omega}\right)\right]_{\mathcal{PD}(G)}\in\mathcal{PD}(G),

(5.53)

with

\left(c,\kappa_{R},\kappa_{L},b,a_{\omega}\right)

in
Lemma
5.12
, independent of the choice of
\left((W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)
.

(ii) of Lemma
5.9
means that
W_{g}^{\epsilon}W_{h}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}{W_{gh}^{\epsilon}}^{*}
and

u_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)}
are proportional.
Taking
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\Gamma_{L}\otimes\Gamma_{R}\right)
of them, we obtain (
2.6
).
Taking
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\Gamma_{L}\otimes\Gamma_{R}\right)
of (
5.47
), we obtain (
2.7
) and (
2.8
).
The condition (
2.9
) can be checked by the repeated use of (
5.47
)
as in the proof of Lemma 2.4 in
[
O4
]
.
Hence we get
\left(c,\kappa_{R},\kappa_{L},b,a_{\omega}\right)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{PD}}(G)
.

To see the last claim (
5.52
), recall that
the ambiguity of
\left((W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)
are just
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-phases.
Therefore, the difference of
c
and
c^{\prime}

is of the form
d^{2}_{a}\sigma
, with some
\sigma\in C^{2}(G,{\mathrm{U}}(1)\oplus{\mathrm{U}}(1))
.
This proves
(
5.52
).

\square

5.4
Well-defined-ness of the index

In this subsection, we show that the index we derived in the previous subsection does not depend
on the choice of the objects we used.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
,
\alpha^{(1)},\alpha^{(2)}\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,

0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\in I(\omega,\theta)
,

(\alpha_{L}^{(i)},\alpha_{R}^{(i)},\Upsilon^{(i)})\in{\mathcal{D}}^{V}(\alpha^{(i)},\theta)
,
i=1,2
.
Then we have

\displaystyle h^{(1)}\left(\omega,\alpha^{(1)},\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L}^{(1)},\alpha_{R}^{(1)},\Upsilon^{(1)})\right)=h^{(1)}\left(\omega,\alpha^{(2)},\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L}^{(2)},\alpha_{R}^{(2)},\Upsilon^{(2)})\right)\in\mathcal{PD}(G).

(5.54)

Definition 5.15
.

From
5.14
, for each

\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
,

0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\in I(\omega,\theta)

we may define

\displaystyle h^{(2)}\left(\omega,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\right):=h^{(1)}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)\in\mathcal{PD}(G),

(5.55)

independent of the choice of
\alpha
,
(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)
.

Because

\displaystyle\omega^{(0)}\left(\alpha_{L}^{(1)}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}^{(1)}\right)\Upsilon^{(1)}\simeq\omega\simeq\omega^{(0)}\left(\alpha_{L}^{(2)}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}^{(2)}\right)\Upsilon^{(2)},

(5.56)

there is a unitary
V\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathcal{H}}_{L}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{R}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits V\circ\left(\pi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\right)\circ\left(\alpha_{L}^{(1)}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}^{(1)}\right)\Upsilon^{(1)}=\left(\pi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\right)\circ\left(\alpha_{L}^{(2)}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}^{(2)}\right)\Upsilon^{(2)}.

(5.57)

As all the automorphisms in the equations are graded, from Lemma
C.1
,
V

is graded with respect to
\Gamma_{L}\otimes\Gamma_{R}
.
Let

\left((W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha^{(1)},\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L}^{(1)},\alpha_{R}^{(1)},\Upsilon^{(1)})\right)
.
As in Lemma 2.11 of
[
O4
]
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(VW_{g}^{\epsilon}V^{*}\right)\circ\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}^{(2)}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}^{(2)}\right)=\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}^{(2)}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}^{(2)}\right)\Upsilon^{(2)}\beta_{g}^{U}{\eta_{g}^{\epsilon}}^{-1}\tau^{-a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}{\Upsilon^{(2)}}^{-1}

(5.58)

We also can check

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\right)V^{*}\right)\left(\pi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\right)\circ\left(\alpha_{L}^{(2)}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}^{(2)}\right)\\
&=\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}^{(2)}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}^{(2)}\beta_{g}^{UR}\left(\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\beta_{h}^{UR}\left(\left(\eta_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)_{-a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\eta_{gh,R}^{\epsilon}\left(\beta_{gh}^{UR}\right)^{-1}.\end{split}

(5.59)

From Lemma
C.1
, this means that there is some

u_{R}^{(2)\epsilon}(g,h)\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{R})
such that

V\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\right)V^{*}=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)\epsilon}(g,h)
.
Similarly,
there is some

u_{L}^{(2)\epsilon}(g,h)\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L})
such that

V\left(u_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)}\right)V^{*}=u_{L}^{(2)\epsilon}(g,h)\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)}
.
From (
5.58
), (
5.59
) and its analog for
u_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)
,
we can see that
((VW_{g}^{\epsilon}V^{*}),(u_{\sigma}^{(2)\epsilon}))

belongs to
\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha^{(2)},\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L}^{(2)},\alpha_{R}^{(2)},\Upsilon^{(2)})\right)
.
As in Lemma 2.11 (2.90) of
[
O4
]
, one can check that

\displaystyle\begin{split}c\left(\omega,\alpha^{(1)},\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L}^{(1)},\alpha_{R}^{(1)},\Upsilon^{(1)}),(W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)=c\left(\omega,\alpha^{(2)},\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}),(\alpha_{L}^{(2)},\alpha_{R}^{(2)},\Upsilon^{(2)}),((VW_{g}^{\epsilon}V^{*}),(u_{\sigma}^{(2)\epsilon}))\right).\end{split}

(5.60)

It is also clear that the grading of
VW_{g}^{\epsilon}V^{*}
,
u_{\sigma}^{(2)\epsilon}

are equal to that of
W_{g}^{\epsilon}
,
u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)
.
Hence we obtain the claim.

\square

Lemma 5.16
.

For any
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
,

0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(1)}),(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(2)})\in I(\omega,\theta)
,
we have

\displaystyle h^{(2)}\left(\omega,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(1)})\right)=h^{(2)}\left(\omega,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(2)})\right).

(5.61)

Definition 5.17
.

From Lemma
5.16
, for each
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
,

0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
we can define

\displaystyle h^{(3)}(\omega,\theta):=h^{(2)}\left(\omega,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})\right),

(5.62)

independent of the choice of
(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon})
.

Fix some

\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
and

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\in{\mathcal{D}}^{V}(\alpha,\theta)
.
Note that
K_{g,\sigma}^{\epsilon}:=\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(2)}\circ{\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(1)}}^{-1}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}\cap H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}}}\right)
.
Set
K_{g}^{\epsilon}=K_{g,L}^{\epsilon}\hat{\otimes}K_{g,R}^{\epsilon}
.
Because

\omega\beta_{g}^{U}\simeq\omega\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\eta_{g}^{\epsilon(1)}\simeq\omega\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\eta_{g}^{\epsilon(2)}
,
we have
\omega\left(K_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)_{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\simeq\omega
.
On the other hand, we have

\omega\simeq\omega^{(0)}\left(\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}\right)\Upsilon
.
Combining these, we obtain

\omega^{(0)}\left(\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}\right)\Upsilon\left(K_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)_{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\simeq\omega^{(0)}\left(\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}\right)\Upsilon
.
Because
K_{g}^{\epsilon}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}}\right)
and
\Upsilon
commute,
we then obtain

\omega^{(0)}\left(\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}\right)\left(K_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)_{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\simeq\omega^{(0)}\left(\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}\right).

Because both sides of this equation are pure and homogeneous,
from Lemma
4.1
, we have

\omega_{\sigma}\alpha_{\sigma}\left(K_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}\right)_{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\simeq\omega_{\sigma}\alpha_{\sigma}.

Hence there is a unitary
V_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}

such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}\right)\pi_{\sigma}=\pi_{\sigma}\alpha_{\sigma}\left(K_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}\right)_{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\alpha_{\sigma}^{-1}.

(5.63)

It is homogeneous because of Lemma
C.1
and
we denote by
m_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}
the grading of
V_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}
,
i.e.,
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\Gamma_{\sigma}\left(V_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}\right)=(-1)^{m_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}}V_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}
.
Fix some
\left((W_{g}^{\epsilon}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h))\right)

in
\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(1)}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)
.
We denote by
\kappa_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)
the grading of
u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)
.
Because
\left(K_{hR}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)_{a(h)\epsilon^{\prime}-a(g)\epsilon}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}\cap H_{R}}\right)

and
\Upsilon
commute, one can show that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{hR}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\right){W_{g}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\right)\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)=\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\beta_{g}^{UR}{\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon(1)}}^{-1}\left(K_{hR}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)_{a(h)\epsilon^{\prime}-a(g)\epsilon}\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon(1)}{\beta_{g}^{UR}}^{-1}.\end{split}

(5.64)

From this, by Lemma
C.1
