, there are unitaries
x_{g,h,R}^{\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{R})

such that

\displaystyle W_{g}^{\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{hR}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\right){W_{g}^{\epsilon}}^{*}=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes x_{g,h,R}^{\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}}.

(5.65)

We also set
v_{gR}^{\epsilon}:=x_{g,g,R}^{\epsilon,\epsilon}
.
Note that this unitary
v_{gR}^{\epsilon}
have the grading
m_{gR}^{\epsilon}

as
V_{gR}^{\epsilon}

with respect to
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\Gamma_{R}
.
Because
\beta_{g}^{UR}{\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon(1)}}^{-1}\left(K_{hR}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)_{a(h)\epsilon^{\prime}-a(g)\epsilon}\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon(1)}{\beta_{g}^{UR}}^{-1}
belongs to

\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}\cap H_{R}^{c_{R}^{(2)}}}\right)
,
we have

\displaystyle x_{g,h,R}^{\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}}\in\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\cap H_{R}^{c^{(2)}_{R}}\right)^{c}\cap H_{R}}\right)^{\prime}.

(5.66)

From Lemma
5.9
(iii),
we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{k}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes x_{g,h,R}^{\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)\in\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{R}).

(5.67)

Therefore, from Lemma
5.9
(ii), we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{k}^{\epsilon}W_{f}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(k)}\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes x_{g,h,R}^{\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(k,f)\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(k.f))}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{kf}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes x_{g,h,R}^{\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}}\right).

(5.68)

Similarly, recalling Lemma
C.1
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\left(V_{hL}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{m_{hL}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}}\right){W_{g}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\right)\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)=\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\beta_{g}^{UL}{\eta_{gL}^{\epsilon(1)}}^{-1}\left(K_{hL}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)_{a(h)\epsilon^{\prime}-a(g)\epsilon}\eta_{gL}^{\epsilon(1)}{\beta_{g}^{UL}}^{-1}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}.\end{split}

(5.69)

Furthermore, by the same argument as (
5.64
), one can also check

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\left(V_{gL}^{\epsilon}\otimes V_{gR}^{\epsilon}\Gamma_{R}^{m_{gL}^{\epsilon}}\right)^{*}\right)\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)=\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)\Upsilon\beta_{g}^{U}{\eta_{g}^{\epsilon(2)}}^{-1}\tau^{-a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\Upsilon^{-1}\end{split}

(5.70)

Using (
5.36
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{g}^{U\sigma}\left(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(2)}\right)^{-1}\beta_{h}^{U\sigma}\left(\left(\eta_{h\sigma}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon(2)}\right)_{-a_{\omega}(g){\epsilon}}\right)^{-1}\eta_{gh,\sigma}^{\epsilon(2)}\left(\beta_{gh}^{U\sigma}\right)^{-1}\\
&=\beta_{g}^{U\sigma}{\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(1)}}^{-1}{K_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}}^{-1}\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(1)}{\beta_{g}^{U\sigma}}^{-1}\\
&\beta_{g}^{U\sigma}\left(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(1)}\right)^{-1}\beta_{h}^{U\sigma}\left(\left(\eta_{h\sigma}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon(1)}\right)_{-a_{\omega}(g){\epsilon}}\right)^{-1}\eta_{gh,\sigma}^{\epsilon(1)}\left(\beta_{gh}^{U\sigma}\right)^{-1}\\
&\beta_{gh}^{U\sigma}{\eta_{gh\sigma}^{\epsilon(1)}}^{-1}\left(\left(K_{h\sigma}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)_{-a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\right)^{-1}K_{gh,\sigma}^{\epsilon}\eta_{gh\sigma}^{\epsilon(1)}{\beta_{gh}^{U\sigma}}^{-1}.\end{split}

(5.71)

Note from (
5.64
) that each lines on the right hand side can be implemented by some unitary
in
\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\right)
,
and we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\beta_{g}^{UR}\left(\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon(2)}\right)^{-1}\beta_{h}^{UR}\left(\left(\eta_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon(2)}\right)_{-a_{\omega}(g){\epsilon}}\right)^{-1}\eta_{gh,R}^{\epsilon(2)}\left(\beta_{gh}^{UR}\right)^{-1}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\begin{gathered}W_{g}^{\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{V_{gR}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\right){{W_{g}}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\right)\\
W_{gh}^{\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{V_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}}^{*}\right){{W_{gh}}^{\epsilon}}^{*}W_{gh}^{\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{ghR}^{\epsilon}\right){{W_{gh}}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\end{gathered}\right)\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\right).\end{split}

(5.72)

By Lemma
C.1
, this means that there is a homogeneous unitary

u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h)
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{R}

such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h)\right)\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}=\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\beta_{g}^{UR}\left(\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon(2)}\right)^{-1}\beta_{h}^{UR}\left(\left(\eta_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon(2)}\right)_{-a_{\omega}(g){\epsilon}}\right)^{-1}\eta_{gh,R}^{\epsilon(2)}\left(\beta_{gh}^{UR}\right)^{-1},

(5.73)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&W_{g}^{\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{V_{gR}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\right){{W_{g}}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\right)W_{gh}^{\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{V_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}}^{*}\right){{W_{gh}}^{\epsilon}}^{*}W_{gh}^{\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{ghR}^{\epsilon}\right){{W_{gh}}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\\
&=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h).\end{split}

(5.74)

Using
v_{gR}^{\epsilon}:=x_{g,g,R}^{\epsilon,\epsilon}
from (
5.65
),
and (
5.68
),
the latter equation can be written

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h)\\
&=(-1)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\kappa_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{v_{gR}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h))}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h))}\right)W_{gh}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{V_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}}^{*}\right)\\
&\quad\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h))}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes v_{ghR}^{\epsilon}\right)\\
&=(-1)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\kappa_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{v_{gR}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h))}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}W_{h}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{V_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}}^{*}\right)\\
&\quad\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h))}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes v_{ghR}^{\epsilon}\right)\\
&=(-1)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)m_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{v_{gR}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{v_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}}^{*}\right)\\
&\quad\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes v_{ghR}^{\epsilon}\right).\end{split}

(5.75)

Similarly we get unitaries
u_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{L}
,
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{L}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h)\right)\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}=\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\beta_{g}^{UL}\left(\eta_{gL}^{\epsilon(2)}\right)^{-1}\beta_{h}^{UL}\left(\left(\eta_{hL}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon(2)}\right)_{-a_{\omega}(g){\epsilon}}\right)^{-1}\eta_{gh,L}^{\epsilon(2)}\left(\beta_{gh}^{UL}\right)^{-1},

(5.76)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&W_{g}^{\epsilon}\left({V_{gL}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{m_{gL}^{\epsilon}}\right){{W_{g}}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\left(u_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon}(g,h)}\right)W_{gh}^{\epsilon}\left({V_{hL}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}}^{*}\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{m_{hL}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}}\right){{W_{gh}}^{\epsilon}}^{*}W_{gh}^{\epsilon}\left(V_{ghL}^{\epsilon}\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{m_{ghL}^{\epsilon}}\right){{W_{gh}}^{\epsilon}}^{*}\\
&=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{L}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h).\end{split}

(5.77)

Hence we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon(2)}:=W_{g}^{\epsilon}\left(V_{gL}^{\epsilon}\otimes V_{gR}^{\epsilon}\Gamma_{R}^{m_{gL}^{\epsilon}}\right)^{*}\right),\left(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h)\right)\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(2)}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right).

(5.78)

We set

\displaystyle\begin{split}b^{(i)}:=b\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(i)}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)\in C^{1}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}),\\
\kappa_{\sigma}^{(i)}:=\kappa_{\sigma}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(i)}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)\in C^{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})\end{split}

(5.79)

for
i=1,2
. (Recall Definition
5.11
and
Definition
5.8
.)
Note that
\kappa_{\sigma}^{(1)}(g,h)=\kappa_{\sigma}(g,h)
.
We also set

\displaystyle\begin{split}c^{(i)}(g,h,k):=c\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(i)}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon),(W_{g}^{\epsilon(i)}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon(i)}(g,h))\right)\end{split}

(5.80)

for
i=1,2
.

We also define
m\in C^{1}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})
by

\displaystyle m(g):=\left(m_{gR}^{+1},m_{gR}^{-1}\right),\quad g\in G.

(5.81)

Note from the definition and (
5.75
), (
5.74
)
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&b^{\epsilon(2)}(g)=b^{\epsilon(1)}(g)+m_{gL}^{\epsilon}+m_{gR}^{\epsilon},\\
&\kappa_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h)=m_{gR}^{\epsilon}+m_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}+m_{ghR}^{\epsilon}+\kappa_{R}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h),\\
&\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h)=m_{gL}^{\epsilon}+m_{hL}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}+m_{gh,L}^{\epsilon}+\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h).\end{split}

(5.82)

in
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
.

Next we would like to derive

c\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon(2)}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon),(W_{g}^{\epsilon(2)}),(u_{\sigma}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h))\right)\in C^{3}(G,{\mathrm{U}}(1)\oplus{\mathrm{U}}(1))
.
To do so, we first calculate
u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h)u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(gh,k)

as follows.
From (
5.75
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{I}_{L}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h)u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(gh,k)\\
&=\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)\cdot\left(m_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(gh,k)\cdot\left(m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{gR}^{\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\right)\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{gh}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(gh,k)v_{ghk,R}^{\epsilon}\right)\\
&=\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)\cdot\left(m_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}+m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(gh,k)\cdot\left(m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{gR}^{\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)}\right)W_{gh}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)\cdot\left[\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)u_{R}^{\epsilon}(gh,k)\right)\right]\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes v_{ghk,R}^{\epsilon}\right).\end{split}

(5.83)

Here in the last equality,
we got an extra sign
\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)\cdot\left(m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}

because we inserted
\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)}
in the middle.
Applyin Lemma
5.10
to
[\cdot]
part,

\displaystyle\begin{split}(\ref {nana46})&=\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)\cdot\left(m_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}+m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(gh,k)\cdot\left(m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\overline{c_{R}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h,k)}\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{gR}^{\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)\left[\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\Gamma_{R}^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)}\right)W_{gh}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)\right]\\
&\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{L}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}}(h,k)\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{L}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,hk)\right)\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes v_{ghk,R}^{\epsilon}\right)\\
&=\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)\cdot\left(m_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}+m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(gh,k)\cdot\left(m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\overline{c_{R}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h,k)}\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{gR}^{\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}W_{h}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{L}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}}(h,k)\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{L}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon}(g,hk)\right)\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes v_{ghk,R}^{\epsilon}\right).\end{split}

(5.84)

In the second equality, we used Lemma
5.9
(ii),(iii)
to
[\cdot]
part.
Now we recall the relation between

u_{R}^{\epsilon}
and
u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}

(
5.75
) and substitute it to the equation above to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}(\ref {hachi46})=&\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)\cdot\left(m_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}+m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(gh,k)\cdot\left(m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,hk)\cdot\left(m_{hkR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)}\\
&\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}(h,k)\cdot\left(m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(h)}(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)}\overline{c_{R}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h,k)}\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(v_{gR}^{\epsilon}\right)^{*}\right)W_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)\left(\begin{gathered}\left(\mathbb{I}_{L}\otimes u_{R}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon(2)}(h,k)\right)\end{gathered}\right)\\
&\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,hk)\right)\\
&=\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)\cdot\left(m_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}+m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(gh,k)\cdot\left(m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,hk)\cdot\left(m_{hkR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)}\\
&\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}(h,k)\cdot\left(m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(-1\right)^{m_{gL}^{\epsilon}\cdot\kappa_{R}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon(2)}(h,k)}\overline{c_{R}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h,k)}\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon(2)}\right)\left(\begin{gathered}\left(\mathbb{I}_{L}\otimes u_{R}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon(2)}(h,k)\right)\end{gathered}\right)\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,hk)\right).\end{split}

(5.85)

In the second equality, we substituted the relation between

W_{g}^{\epsilon}
and

W_{g}^{\epsilon(2)}
(
5.78
).
Now seting
\tilde{\sigma}\in C^{2}(G,{\mathrm{U}}(1)\oplus{\mathrm{U}}(1))
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h):=(-1)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)m_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}},\quad\epsilon=\pm 1,g,h\in G

(5.86)

with (
5.82
)
the phase factor in the last part of equation
can be written as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)\cdot\left(m_{hR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}+m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(gh,k)\cdot\left(m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,hk)\cdot\left(m_{hkR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}\right)}\\
&\left(-1\right)^{\kappa_{L}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon(1)}(h,k)\cdot\left(m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}\right)}\left(-1\right)^{m_{gL}^{\epsilon}\cdot\kappa_{R}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon(2)}(h,k)}\\
&=\frac{\tilde{\sigma}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}(h,k)\cdot\tilde{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,hk)}{\tilde{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(g,h)\cdot\tilde{\sigma}^{\epsilon}(gh,k)}(-1)^{\kappa_{L}^{\epsilon(1)}(g,h)\cdot m_{kR}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(gh)}\epsilon}}\left(-1\right)^{m_{gL}^{\epsilon}\cdot\kappa_{R}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon(2)}(h,k)}\\
&=\left(d^{2}_{a_{\omega}}\tilde{\sigma}(g,h,k)\cdot(-1)^{\kappa_{L}^{(1)}(g,h)\cdot\left(m^{a_{\omega}(gh)}(k)\right)}(-1)^{\left(\left({\kappa_{R}^{(2)}}^{a_{\omega}(g)}(h,k)\right)\right)\cdot\left(b^{(2)}(g)-b^{(1)}(g)-m(g)\right)}\right)_{\epsilon}\end{split}

(5.87)

Hence we got

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{I}_{L}\otimes u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,h)u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(gh,k)\\
&=\left(d^{2}_{a_{\omega}}\tilde{\sigma}(g,h,k)\cdot(-1)^{\kappa_{L}^{(1)}(g,h)\cdot\left(m^{a_{\omega}(gh)}(k)\right)}(-1)^{\left(\left({\kappa_{R}^{(2)}}^{a_{\omega}(g)}(h,k)\right)\right)\cdot\left(b^{(2)}(g)-b^{(1)}(g)-m(g)\right)}\overline{c_{R}^{(1)}(g,h,k)}\right)_{\epsilon}\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{\epsilon(2)}\right)\left(\begin{gathered}\left(\mathbb{I}_{L}\otimes u_{R}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon(2)}(h,k)\right)\end{gathered}\right)\mathbb{I}\otimes\left(u_{R}^{\epsilon(2)}(g,hk)\right)\end{split}

(5.88)

Hence we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}{c_{R}^{(2)}(g,h,k)}=d^{2}_{a_{\omega}}\tilde{\sigma}(g,h,k)\cdot(-1)^{\kappa_{L}^{(1)}(g,h)\cdot\left(m^{a_{\omega}(gh)}(k)\right)}(-1)^{\left(\left({\kappa_{R}^{(2)}}^{a_{\omega}(g)}(h,k)\right)\right)\cdot\left(b^{(2)}(g)-b^{(1)}(g)-m(g)\right)}{c_{R}^{(1)}(g,h,k)}.\end{split}

(5.89)

Combining this with (
5.82
), we have

\displaystyle(c^{(1)},\kappa_{R}^{(1)},\kappa_{L}^{(1)},b^{(1)},a_{\omega})\sim_{\mathcal{PD}(G)}(c^{(2)},\kappa_{R}^{(2)},\kappa_{L}^{(2)},b^{(2)},a_{\omega})

(5.90)

with
m\in C^{1}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})
in (
5.81
)
and
\tilde{\sigma}\in C^{2}(G,{\mathrm{U}}(1)\oplus{\mathrm{U}}(1))
in (
5.86
).
This proves the Lemma.

\square

Definition 5.18
.

Let
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
.
By the same simple argument as Lemma 2.17 in
[
O4
]
,
one can show that
h^{(3)}(\omega,\theta)
is independent of the choice of

0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
.
Hence we can define an index
h(\omega)=h^{(3)}(\omega,\theta)
,
independent of the choice of
\theta
.

6
Stability

Now, in order to prove Theorem
2.6
, it suffices to prove the following.

Theorem 6.1
.

For any
\omega\in\mathop{\mathrm{SPT}}\nolimits
,
\gamma\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits_{\beta}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}})
,
we have
h(\omega)=h(\omega\circ\gamma)
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,
0<\theta_{0}<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,

(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}{})\in I(\omega,\theta_{0})
,

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\in{\mathcal{D}}^{V}(\alpha,\theta)
.
Set
\theta_{2}:=\theta
and choose

\displaystyle 0<\theta_{0}<\theta_{0.8}<\theta_{1}<\theta_{1.2}<\theta_{1.8}<\theta_{2}<\theta_{2.2}<\theta_{2.8}<\theta_{3}<\theta_{3.2}<\frac{\pi}{2}.

(6.1)

Because
\gamma\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits_{\beta}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}})
, by the same proof as
Theorem 5.2
[
O4
]
,
we can decompose

\gamma
as

\displaystyle\gamma=\gamma_{C}\circ\gamma_{H}.

(6.2)

Here,
automorphisms

\gamma_{H}
and

\gamma_{C}

belong to

\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\right)

and
allow the following decompositions:

1.
\gamma_{H}
is decomposed as

\displaystyle\gamma_{H}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\gamma_{H,{L}}\hat{\otimes}\gamma_{H,R}\right)=({\rm{inner}})\circ\gamma_{0}

(6.3)

with
some
\gamma_{H,{\sigma}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\left(C_{\theta_{0}}\right)\cap H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}^{(-1)}}}}\right)
,
\sigma=L,R
.
Here set
\gamma_{0}:=\gamma_{H,{L}}\hat{\otimes}\gamma_{H,R}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{0}}}}\right)
,
and
c_{R}^{(-1)}=6
,
c_{L}^{(-1)}:=-6
.
We have chosen the support of

\gamma_{H,{\sigma}}
as
C_{\theta_{0}}\cap H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}^{(-1)}}

so that
\widetilde{\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}{}}

in (
6.14
) below has a support in
C_{\theta_{0}}\cap H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}}
.

2.
The automorphism

\gamma_{C}
allows a
decomposition

\displaystyle\gamma_{C}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\gamma_{CS}

\displaystyle\gamma_{CS}=\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}{\hat{\otimes}}\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}{\hat{\otimes}}\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}{\hat{\otimes}}\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}{\hat{\otimes}}\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}{\hat{\otimes}}\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)

(6.4)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\gamma_{X}:=\widehat{\bigotimes}_{\sigma=L,R,\zeta=D,U}\gamma_{X,\sigma,\zeta},\quad\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}:=\widehat{\bigotimes}_{\sigma=L,R}\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma},\quad\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}:=\widehat{\bigotimes}_{\zeta=D,U}\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}],\zeta},\\
&\gamma_{X,\sigma,\zeta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{X}\cap H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}}\cap H_{\zeta}}\right),\quad\gamma_{X,\sigma}:=\widehat{\bigotimes}_{\zeta=U,D}\gamma_{X,\sigma,\zeta},\quad\gamma_{X,\zeta}:=\widehat{\bigotimes}_{\sigma=L,R}\gamma_{X,\sigma,\zeta},\\
&\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\cap H_{\sigma}^{c_{\sigma}}}\right),\quad\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}],\zeta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\cap H_{\zeta}}\right),\end{split}

(6.5)

for

\displaystyle X=(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}],(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}],(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}],\quad\sigma=L,R,\quad\zeta=D,U.

(6.6)

These automorphisms satisfy

\displaystyle\gamma_{I}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}=\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\gamma_{I}\;\quad\text{for all}\quad g\in G,\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma_{I}\circ\tau=\tau\circ\gamma_{I}

(6.7)

for any

\displaystyle I=[0,\theta_{1}],(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}],\left(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}\right],(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}],(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}].

(6.8)

Now we set

\displaystyle\hat{\Upsilon}:=\Upsilon\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{1.8}}^{c}}\right)\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{1.2}}^{c}}\right),

(6.9)

and

\displaystyle\hat{\alpha}_{\sigma}:=\alpha_{\sigma}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}\hat{\otimes}\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],\sigma}\right)\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}\circ\gamma_{H,\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}}),\quad\sigma=L,R.

(6.10)

Then as in the proof of Theorem 3.1 of
[
O4
]

Step 1
, we can check

\displaystyle\alpha\circ\gamma=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\hat{\alpha}_{L}\otimes\hat{\alpha}_{R}\right)\circ\hat{\Upsilon}.

(6.11)

Therefore, we have

(\hat{\alpha}_{L},\hat{\alpha}_{R},\hat{\Upsilon})\in{\mathcal{D}}^{V}\left(\alpha\gamma,\theta_{1.2}\right)
.
Because
\tau
and
\gamma_{CS}
commute,
we also have

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}\tau^{a}\gamma=\tau^{a}\left(\left(\gamma_{HL}\right)_{-a}^{-1}\gamma_{HL}\hat{\otimes}\left(\gamma_{HR}\right)_{-a}^{-1}\gamma_{HR}\right)\circ({\rm{inner}})

(6.12)

As in the proof of Theorem 3.1 of
[
O4
]

Step 2

we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\gamma^{-1}\eta_{g}^{\epsilon}\gamma=\gamma_{0}^{-1}\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}^{-1}\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}^{-1}\eta_{g}^{\epsilon}\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\gamma_{0}\circ({\rm{inner}}),\\
&\gamma^{-1}{\beta_{g}^{U}}^{-1}\gamma\beta_{g}^{U}=\gamma_{0}^{-1}{\beta_{g}^{U}}^{-1}\gamma_{0}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ({\rm{inner}}).\end{split}

(6.13)

Set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}{}}:=\left(\gamma_{H_{\sigma}}\right)_{-a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}^{-1}\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]\sigma}\right)^{-1}\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]\sigma}\right)^{-1}\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}{}\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]\sigma}\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{U\sigma}\right)^{-1}\gamma_{H_{\sigma}}\beta_{g}^{U\sigma}.

(6.14)

We claim
(\widetilde{\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}{}})\in I(\omega\gamma,\theta_{1.2})
.
It can be seen as follows using (
6.12
), (
6.13
) :

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\omega\gamma\beta_{g}^{U}=\omega\beta_{g}^{U}{\beta_{g}^{U}}^{-1}\gamma\beta_{g}^{U}=\omega\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\left({\eta_{gL}^{\epsilon}{}}\hat{\otimes}{\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon}{}}\right){\beta_{g}^{U}}^{-1}\gamma\beta_{g}^{U}\circ({\rm{inner}})\\
&=\omega\gamma\gamma^{-1}\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\gamma\circ\gamma^{-1}\left({\eta_{gL}^{\epsilon}{}}\hat{\otimes}{\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon}{}}\right)\gamma\circ\gamma^{-1}{\beta_{g}^{U}}^{-1}\gamma\beta_{g}^{U}\circ({\rm{inner}})=\omega\gamma\tau^{a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\left(\widetilde{\eta_{gL}^{\epsilon}{}}\hat{\otimes}\widetilde{\eta_{gR}^{\epsilon}{}}\right)\circ({\rm{inner}}).\end{split}

(6.15)

From this equality, we can also see that
a_{\omega}(g)=a_{\omega\gamma}(g)

for any
g\in G
.

Because
(\hat{\alpha}_{L},\hat{\alpha}_{R},\hat{\Upsilon})\in{\mathcal{D}}^{V}\left(\alpha\gamma,\theta_{1.2}\right)

and
(\widetilde{\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}{}})\in I(\omega\gamma,\theta_{1.2})
,
in order to prove the Theorem,
it suffices to show

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta_{2},\left(\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}{}\right),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Upsilon)\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega\gamma,\alpha\gamma,\theta_{1.2},\left(\widetilde{\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}{}}\right),(\hat{\alpha}_{L},\hat{\alpha}_{R},\hat{\Upsilon})\right).\end{split}

(6.16)

In other words,
it suffices to show the following:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(\hat{\alpha}_{L}\hat{\otimes}\hat{\alpha}_{R}\right)\hat{\Upsilon}\beta_{g}^{U}\left(\widetilde{\eta}_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\tau^{-a_{\omega\gamma}(g)\epsilon}\hat{\Upsilon}^{-1}\left(\hat{\alpha}_{L}\hat{\otimes}\hat{\alpha}_{R}\right)^{-1}=\left(\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}\right)\Upsilon\beta_{g}^{U}\left(\eta_{g}^{\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\tau^{-a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\Upsilon^{-1}\left(\alpha_{L}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{R}\right)^{-1},\\
&\hat{\alpha}_{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{U\sigma}\left(\widetilde{\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}{}}\right)^{-1}\beta_{h}^{U\sigma}\left(\left(\widetilde{\eta_{h\sigma}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}{}}\right)_{-a_{\omega\gamma}(g)\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\widetilde{\eta_{gh\sigma}^{\epsilon}{}}{\beta_{gh}^{U\sigma}}^{-1}\hat{\alpha}_{\sigma}^{-1}\\
&=\alpha_{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{U\sigma}\left({\eta_{g\sigma}^{\epsilon}{}}\right)^{-1}\beta_{h}^{U\sigma}\left(\left({\eta_{h\sigma}^{(-1)^{a_{\omega}(g)}\epsilon}{}}\right)_{-a_{\omega}(g)\epsilon}\right)^{-1}{\eta_{gh\sigma}^{\epsilon}{}}{\beta_{gh}^{U\sigma}}^{-1}\alpha_{\sigma}^{-1}\end{split}

(6.17)

This can be shown as in
Step 3.
of Theorem 3.1
[
O4
]
,
noting the commutativity (
6.7
).

\square

7
Automorphisms on
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)

In this section we show the following proposition.
It allows us to reduce the support of homogeneous automorphisms
by finite portion.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}
be a finite even dimensional Hilbert space with a complex conjugation
{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
be an infinite dimensional Hilbert space with a complex conjugation
{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}
.
Set
{\mathfrak{K}}:={\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
with a complex conjugation
{\mathfrak{C}}={\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}
.
Then for any
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)\right)
,
there are unitary
u\in{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)
and an automorphism

\alpha_{2}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)\right)
such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits u\circ\alpha=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{2}.

(7.1)

We start by several Lemmas.

Lemma 7.2
.

For each
n\in 2{\mathbb{N}}
, there is some
\delta_{n}>0

satisfying the following condition.:
Let
{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
be Hilbert spaces with complex conjugations
{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}
.
Suppose that
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}
is
n
-dimensional.
Suppose that

{\mathfrak{K}}_{3}
is even-finite-dimensional, and
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
is infinite dimensional.
Set
{\mathfrak{K}}:={\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{3}

with a complex conjugation
{\mathfrak{C}}={\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{3}
.
Let
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)\right)
.
Let
\{e_{IJ}\}_{I,J\subset\{1,\ldots,\frac{\dim{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}{2}\}}

be a system of matrix units spanning
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)

with grading of
e_{IJ}
being
|I|+|J|\mod 2
.
Suppose that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(\alpha(e_{IJ}),{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{3},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{3}\right)\right)<\delta_{n}.

(7.2)

Here
\mathrm{d}(X,Y)
means the distance between the two subsets

X,Y
of
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)
, with respect to the
C^{*}
-norm.
Then there is an even unitary
u\in{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u\right)\left(\alpha\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)\right)\right)\subset{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{3},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{3}\right).

(7.3)

This holds directly from the proof of Lemma III 3.2, 3.2
of
[
D
]
.
It is easy to see that the unitary obtained by the argument there
is even.

\square

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
{\mathfrak{K}}_{i}
,
i=1,2
be finite even dimensional Hilbert spaces with
complex conjugations
{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}
.
Let
\{e_{IJ}\}_{I,J\subset\{1,\ldots,\frac{\dim{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}{2}\}}

be a system of matrix units spanning
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)

with grading of
e_{IJ}
being
|I|+|J|\mod 2
.
Let
\{f_{IJ}\}_{I,J\subset\{1,\ldots,\frac{\dim{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}{2}\}}

be a system of matrix units in
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)

with grading of
f_{IJ}
being
|I|+|J|\mod 2
.
Suppose that there is a self-adjoint unitary
v\in{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)^{(1)}

such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(v)(f_{IJ})=(-1)^{|I|+|J|}f_{IJ}
.
Then there is an even unitary
U\in{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)

such that
f_{IJ}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U\right)(e_{IJ})
.

Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{1},\pi_{1})
be
Fock representations of
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)
with
grading unitary
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}
.
For each
I\subset\{1,\ldots,\frac{\dim{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}{2}\}
,
note that
\pi_{1}(e_{II}){\mathcal{H}}_{1}
is one-dimensional
space because
\pi_{1}(e_{II})
is a minimal projection of
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)

and
\pi_{1}
is irreducible.
Let

e_{I}
be a unit vector in
\pi_{1}(e_{II}){\mathcal{H}}_{1}
.
Because
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}\pi_{1}(e_{II}){\mathcal{H}}_{1}=\pi_{1}(e_{II})\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}{\mathcal{H}}_{1}=\pi_{1}(e_{II}){\mathcal{H}}_{1}
,

e_{I}
is eigenvector of
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}
with eigenvalue
\pm 1
.
If
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}e_{\emptyset}=e_{\emptyset}
/
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}e_{\emptyset}=-e_{\emptyset}
, set

\Gamma_{1}:=\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}
/
\Gamma_{1}:=-\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}
.
so that
\Gamma_{1}e_{\emptyset}=e_{\emptyset}
.
For general
I\subset\{1,\ldots,\frac{\dim{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}{2}\}
,
we have
e_{I}=c_{I}\pi_{1}(e_{I\emptyset})e_{\emptyset}
, with some
c_{I}\in{\mathrm{U}}(1)
.
Hence we have

\Gamma_{1}e_{I}=c_{I}\Gamma_{1}\pi_{1}(e_{I\emptyset})e_{\emptyset}=(-1)^{|I|}c_{I}\pi_{1}(e_{I\emptyset})e_{\emptyset}=(-1)^{|I|}e_{I}
.

Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{2},\pi_{2})
be
Fock representations of
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)
with
grading unitary
\Gamma_{2}
.
Then
({\mathcal{H}},\pi):=({\mathcal{H}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{2},\pi_{1}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{2})

is a Fock representation of
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)

with a grading unitary
\Gamma:=\Gamma_{1}\otimes\Gamma_{2}
.

Setting
{\mathcal{K}}:=\pi(f_{\emptyset\emptyset}){\mathcal{H}}
,

\displaystyle W\xi:=\sum_{I\subset\{1,\ldots,\frac{\dim{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}{2}\}}e_{I}\otimes\pi(f_{\emptyset I})\xi,\quad\xi\in{\mathcal{H}}

(7.4)

defines a unitary from
{\mathcal{H}}
to
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{K}}
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W\right)\left(\pi(f_{IJ})\right)=e_{IJ}\otimes\mathbb{I}.

(7.5)

We also have

\displaystyle W\Gamma=\left(\Gamma_{1}\otimes\Gamma\pi\left(f_{\emptyset\emptyset}\right)\right)W.

(7.6)

Because
f_{\emptyset\emptyset}
is even,

{\mathcal{K}}
is invariant under
\Gamma
and
\Gamma\pi\left(f_{\emptyset\emptyset}\right)

is a self-adjoint unitary on
{\mathcal{K}}
.
We have

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\pi(v)\right)\left(\Gamma\pi\left(f_{\emptyset\emptyset}\right)\right)=-\Gamma\pi\left(f_{\emptyset\emptyset}\right)
,
with the unitary
v
.
It means the eigenvalue
1
of
\pi(\Gamma)\pi\left(f_{\emptyset\emptyset}\right)

and that of
-1
have the same degeneracy.
From this, we may find a unitary

u:{\mathcal{H}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{K}}

such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits u\left(\Gamma_{2}\right)=\Gamma\pi\left(f_{\emptyset\emptyset}\right)
.
Setting
\tilde{U}:=\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{1}}\otimes u^{*}\right)W\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}})
,
we have

\tilde{U}\Gamma=\Gamma\tilde{U}.

Then there is an even unitary
U\in{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)
such that
\tilde{U}=\pi(U^{*})
.
From the definition, we can check that
f_{IJ}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U\right)(e_{IJ})
.

\square

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}
be a finite even dimensional Hilbert space with a complex conjugation
{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
be an infinite dimensional Hilbert space with a complex conjugation
{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}
.
Set
{\mathfrak{K}}:={\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
with a complex conjugation
{\mathfrak{C}}={\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}
.
Let
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)\right)
such that
\alpha|_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)}
.
Then there is an automorphism
\alpha_{2}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)\right)

such that
\alpha=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)}\hat{\otimes}\alpha_{2}
.

It suffices to show
\alpha\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)\right)={\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)
.
Because
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}
is of finite even dimensional and
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}

is of infinite dimensional they are
*
-isomorphic to some CAR-algebras.
Let
v_{{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}\in{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)
be the grading operator
of
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)
. It is an even self-adjoint unitary.
From
[
AM
]
Lemma 4.15,
we have

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)={\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)^{(0)}+v_{{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)^{(1)}.

(7.7)

For any
f\in{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
, we claim
\alpha\left(B(f)\right)

belongs to
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)
.
To see this, note because
\alpha
is graded, that
\alpha\left(B(f)\right)

is odd.
Furthermore, any homogeneous
a\in{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle a\alpha\left(B(f)\right)-(-1)^{\partial a}\alpha\left(B(f)\right)a=\alpha\left(aB(f)-(-1)^{\partial a}B(f)a\right)=0,

(7.8)

because
\alpha(a)=a
from the assumption.
Hence we see that
\alpha\left(B(f)\right)v_{{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}

is an odd element in
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)^{\prime}\cap{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)
.
From (
7.7
), we conclude
\alpha(B(f))\in{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)^{(1)}
, proving the claim.

As this holds for any
f\in{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
, we conclude

\alpha\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)\right)\subset{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)
.
The same argument for
\alpha^{-1}
implies the opposite inclusion and we obtain

\alpha\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)\right)={\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)
.

\square

Let
{\mathfrak{K}}_{0}
be a
2
-dimensional
{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}
-invariant subspace of

{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
with the complex conjugation

{\mathfrak{C}}_{0}:=\left.{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right|_{{\mathfrak{K}}_{0}}
.
Let
\{e_{IJ}\}_{I,J\subset\{1,\ldots,\frac{\dim{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}{2}+1\}}

be a system of matrix units spanning
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{0},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{0}\right)

with grading of
e_{IJ}
being
|I|+|J|\mod 2
.
Let
\{E_{IJ}\}_{I,J\subset\{1,\ldots,\frac{\dim{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}{2}\}}

be a system of matrix units spanning
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)

with grading of
E_{IJ}
being
|I|+|J|\mod 2
.

We claim that there is an even finite dimensional
{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}
-invariant subspace

{\mathfrak{K}}_{3}
of
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\ominus{\mathfrak{K}}_{0}

with the complex conjugation
{\mathfrak{C}}_{3}:={\mathfrak{C}}_{2}|_{{\mathfrak{K}}_{3}}
,
and a even unitary
U\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)\right)

such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits U\circ\alpha\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{0},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{0}\right)\right)\subset{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{3},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{3}\right).

(7.9)

To show this, let
\delta_{{\dim{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}+2}
be the number given in Lemma
7.2
.
Then there exists an even finite dimensional
{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}
-invariant subspace

{\mathfrak{K}}_{3}
of
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\ominus{\mathfrak{K}}_{0}

such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(\alpha\left(e_{IJ}\right),{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{3},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{3}\right)\right)<\delta_{{\dim{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}+2}.

(7.10)

Applying Lemma
7.2

with
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}
,
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
,
{\mathfrak{K}}_{3}

replaced by
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{0}
,
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}\ominus\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{3}\right)
,

{\mathfrak{K}}_{3}

we obtain an even
U\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)\right)
satisfying (
7.9
).

Next we apply Lemma
7.3

with
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}
,
{\mathfrak{K}}_{2}
,
e_{IJ}

f_{IJ}

replaced by
{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}

{\mathfrak{K}}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{3}
,
E_{IJ}

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits U\circ\alpha(E_{IJ})
.
Note that because
U
is even and
\alpha
is homogeneous,

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits U\circ\alpha(E_{IJ})
has a degree
|I|+|J|\mod 2
.
The algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{0},{\mathfrak{C}}_{0}\right)
has an odd self-adjoint unitary
v_{0}
.
Because
U
is even and
\alpha
is homogeneous,
v:=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits U\circ\alpha(v_{0})

is an odd self-adjoint unitary in
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{3},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{3}\right)
.
It satisfies

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits v\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits U\circ\alpha(e_{IJ})\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits U\circ\alpha\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(v_{0})(e_{IJ})\right)=(-1)^{|I|+|J|}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits U\circ\alpha(e_{IJ}).

(7.11)

Hence the required conditions of Lemma
7.3
is satisfied.
Applying Lemma
7.3
, we obtain
an even unitary
V\in{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{K}}_{3},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{C}}_{3}\right)

such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits V(E_{IJ})=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits U\circ\alpha(E_{IJ})
.
Setting
u:=V^{*}U\in{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)
,

u
is a even unitary such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits u\circ\alpha(e_{IJ})=e_{IJ}

for all
I,J\subset\{1,\ldots,\frac{\dim{\mathfrak{K}}_{1}}{2}\}
.
It means
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits u\circ\alpha|_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{1},{\mathfrak{C}}_{1}\right)}
.
By Lemma
7.4
it means
an automorphism

\alpha_{2}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{S}DC}}\left({\mathfrak{K}}_{2},{\mathfrak{C}}_{2}\right)\right)
satisfying (
7.1
).

\square

The author is grateful to Yuji Tachikawa for a stimulating discussion and helpful comments.

It was also supported by JST CREST Grant Number JPMJCR19T2.

Appendix A
Basic Notations

For a Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}
,
B({\mathcal{H}})
denotes the set of all bounded operators on
{\mathcal{H}}
,
while
{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}})
denotes the set of all unitaries on
{\mathcal{H}}
.
If
V:{\mathcal{H}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
is a linear map from a Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
to
another Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
,
then
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(V):B({\mathcal{H}}_{1})\to B({\mathcal{H}}_{2})
denotes the map

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(V)(x):=VxV^{*}
,
x\in B({\mathcal{H}}_{1})
.
Occasionally we write
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{V}
instead of
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(V)
.
For a
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
and
v\in{\mathcal{B}}
, we set

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(v)(x):=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{v}(x):=vxv^{*}
,
x\in{\mathcal{B}}
.

For a state
\omega
on a
C^{*}
-algebra

{\mathfrak{B}}
,
we denote by
({\mathcal{H}}_{\omega},\pi_{\omega},\Omega_{\omega})

its GNS triple.
For a
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathfrak{B}}
, we denote by
{\mathcal{U}}({\mathfrak{B}})

the set of all unitaries in
{\mathfrak{B}}
.
For a
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathfrak{B}}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}_{+,1}

denotes the set of all positive elements of
{\mathfrak{B}}
with
norm less than or equal to
1
.
For states
\omega,\varphi
on a
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathfrak{B}}
,
we write
\omega\simeq\varphi
if they are equivalent
and
\omega\sim_{\mathop{q.e.}}\varphi
if they are quasi-equivalent.
We denote by
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits{\mathcal{B}}
the group of automorphisms on a
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
.
The group of inner automorphisms on a unital
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
is denoted by
\mathop{\mathrm{Inn}}\nolimits{\mathcal{B}}
.
For
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})
,
\gamma_{1}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\gamma_{2}

means there is some unitary
u
in
{\mathcal{B}}
such that
\gamma_{1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(u)\circ\gamma_{2}
.
For a unital
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
, the unit of
{\mathcal{B}}
is denoted by
\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{B}}}
.
For a Hilbert space we write
\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}}:=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})}
.
For a unital
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
, by
{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{B}})
, we mean
the set of all unitary elements in
{\mathcal{B}}
.For a state
\varphi
on
{\mathcal{B}}
and a
C^{*}
-subalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
of
{\mathcal{B}}
,

\varphi|_{{\mathcal{C}}}
indicates the restriction of
\varphi
to
{\mathcal{C}}
.

The center of a von Neumann algebra
{\mathcal{M}}
is denoted by
Z({\mathcal{M}})
.

To denote the composition of automorphisms
\alpha_{1}
,

\alpha_{2}
, all of

\alpha_{1}\circ\alpha_{2}
,

\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}
,
\alpha_{1}\cdot\alpha_{2}

are used.
Frequently, the first one serves as a bracket
to visually separate a group of operators.

Appendix B
Graded von Neumann algebras

In this section we collect facts we use about graded von Neumann algebras.
See
[
BO
]
for further explanation.
A graded von Neumann algebra is a pair
({\mathcal{M}},\theta)
with

{\mathcal{M}}
a von Neumann algebra
\theta
an involutive automorphism on
{\mathcal{M}}
,

\theta^{2}=\mathrm{Id}
.
The even/odd part of
{\mathcal{M}}
with respect to the grading is denoted by
{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}
/
{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}
.
If
{\mathcal{M}}\subset{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
and there is a self-adjoint unitary

\Gamma
on
{\mathcal{H}}
such that
\mathrm{Ad}_{\Gamma}|_{\mathcal{M}}=\theta
, then
we call
({\mathcal{M}},\theta)
a spatially graded von Neumann algebra with
grading operator
\Gamma
.
We say
({\mathcal{M}},\theta)

is balanced if
{\mathcal{M}}
contains an odd self-adjoint unitary.
If
Z({\mathcal{M}})\cap{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}={\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}
for
the center
Z({\mathcal{M}})
of
{\mathcal{M}}
, we say
({\mathcal{M}},\theta)
is central.

For a homogeneous state
\omega
on a graded
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathfrak{B}}
,
there is a self-adjoint unitary
\Gamma_{\omega}
implementing the grading with respect
to
\pi_{\omega}
.
As a result, the grading extends to the von Neumann algebra

\pi_{\omega}({\mathfrak{B}})^{\prime\prime}
by
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\Gamma_{\omega}
.
We always consider this extension without mentioning explicitly.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
({\mathcal{M}},\Gamma)
be a balanced central graded von Neumann algebra on a Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}
.
Then both of
{\mathcal{M}}
and
{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}
are either a factor or a direct sum of two factors of the same type.

The proof is basically the same as part of Proposition 2.9 of
[
BO
]
.
From Lemma A.2 of
[
BO
]
,
if
{\mathcal{M}}
is not a factor,
it is a direct sum of two factors of the same type
which maps to each other by
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\Gamma
.

Let
U
be a self-adjoint odd unitary in
{\mathcal{M}}
.
Suppose that
{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}
is not a factor.
Then there exists a projection
z
in
Z({\mathcal{M}}^{(0)})
which is not

0
nor
\mathbb{I}
.
For such a projection, we have
z+\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{U}(z)\in{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}\cap\left({{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}}\right)^{\prime}\cap\{U\}^{\prime}=Z({\mathcal{M}})\cap{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}={\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}
,
which then implies that
z+\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{U}(z)=\mathbb{I}
.
(We note
that for orthogonal projections

p,q
satisfying
p+q=t\mathbb{I}
with
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
, either
p+q=\mathbb{I}
or
p=0,\,\mathbb{I}
holds,
by considering the spectrum of
p=t\mathbb{I}-q
.)

We claim
Z({\mathcal{M}}^{(0)})={\mathbb{C}}z+{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}
.
Now, for any projection
s
in
Z({\mathcal{M}}^{(0)})
,

zs
is a projection in
Z({\mathcal{M}}^{(0)})
. Therefore either
zs=0
or
zs+\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{U}(zs)=\mathbb{I}
.
The latter is possible only if
zs=z
because
z+\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{U}(z)=\mathbb{I}
.
Similarly, we have
(\mathbb{I}-z)s=0
or
(\mathbb{I}-z)s=\mathbb{I}-z
.
Hence we have
Z({\mathcal{M}}^{(0)})={\mathbb{C}}z+{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}
, proving the claim.

Hence
{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}
is a summation of two factors
{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}={\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}z\oplus{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}(\mathbb{I}-z)
.
Because
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits U(z)=\mathbb{I}-z
,
these factors
{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}z
,
{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}(\mathbb{I}-z)
are isomorphic.
In particular, they have the same type.

\square

Let
({\mathcal{M}}_{1},\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{\Gamma_{1}})
and
({\mathcal{M}}_{2},\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{\Gamma_{2}})
be spatially
graded von Neumann algebras acting on
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}

with grading operators
\Gamma_{1}
,
\Gamma_{2}
.
We define a product and involution on the algebraic tensor product
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\odot{\mathcal{M}}_{2}

by

\displaystyle(a_{1}\hat{\otimes}b_{1})(a_{2}\hat{\otimes}b_{2})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\partial b_{1}\partial a_{2}}(a_{1}a_{2}\hat{\otimes}b_{1}b_{2}),

\displaystyle(a\hat{\otimes}b)^{*}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\partial a\partial b}a^{*}\hat{\otimes}b^{*}.

(B.1)

for homogeneous elementary tensors.
The algebraic tensor product with this multiplication and involution
is a
*
-algebra, denoted
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\,\hat{\odot}\,{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
.
On the Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\pi(a\hat{\otimes}b):=a\Gamma_{1}^{\partial b}\otimes b

(B.2)

for homogeneous
a\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}
,
b\in{\mathcal{M}}_{2}

defines a faithful
*
-representation of
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\,\hat{\odot}\,{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
.
We call the von Neumann algebra generated by
\pi({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\,\hat{\odot}\,{\mathcal{M}}_{2})

the graded tensor product of

({\mathcal{M}}_{1},{\mathcal{H}}_{1},\Gamma_{1})
and
({\mathcal{M}}_{2},{\mathcal{H}}_{2},\Gamma_{2})

and denote it by
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
.
It is simple to check that

{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
is a spatially graded von Neumann algebra with a grading operator

\Gamma_{1}\otimes\Gamma_{2}
.

For
a\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}
and homogeneous
b\in{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
,
we denote
\pi(a\hat{\otimes}b)
by
a{\hat{\otimes}}b
, embedding
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\,\hat{\odot}\,{\mathcal{M}}_{2}

in
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
.
Note that
\partial(a\hat{\otimes}b)=\partial(a)+\partial(b)
for homogeneous

a\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}
and
b\in{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
.

Lemma B.2
.

For each
i=1,2
,
let
({\mathcal{M}}_{i},\Gamma_{i})
be
balanced central graded von Neumann algebras on a Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
.
Suppose that
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
is of type I factor.
Then both of
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
are type I.

By Lemma
B.1
, all of

{\mathcal{M}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}

are either a factor or a direct sum of two factors of the same type.
Then by Lemma A.1 of
[
BO
]
, the type of
{\mathcal{M}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{i}^{(0)}

are the same for each
i=1,2
.

Because
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
is a type I factor,
it has a faithful semifinite normal trace
\tau
whose restriction
to
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}
is also a faithful semifinite normal trace.
Therefore, from Theorem 2.15 of
[
T
]
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}
is semifinite.
From Theorem 2.30, it means both of
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}
are semifinite.
Because
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
is a type I factor,
for the set of orthogonal projections
{\mathcal{P}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}\right)
of

{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
,

\tau\left({\mathcal{P}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\hat{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}\right)\right)

is a countable set.
It means that for the set of orthogonal projections
{\mathcal{P}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}\right)
of

{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}
,

\tau\left({\mathcal{P}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}\hat{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}\right)\right)

is also countable.
It means that
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}
is not of type II.
It means both of
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}
are type I, hence
both of
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
are type I.

\square

Lemma B.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{H}}
be a Hilbert space with a self-adjoint unitary
\Gamma

that gives a grading for
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
.
Let
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
be
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{\Gamma}
-invariant
type I von Neumann subalgebras of
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
with
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\vee{\mathcal{M}}_{2}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
.
Suppose with respect to the grading given by
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{\Gamma}

that both of
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
have a center of the form

Z\left({\mathcal{M}}_{i}\right)={\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}+{\mathbb{C}}V_{i}
with a self-adjoint odd unitary
V_{i}
.
Suppose further that

\displaystyle ab-(-1)^{\partial a\partial b}ba=0,\quad\text{for homogeneous}\quad a\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1},b\in{\mathcal{M}}_{2}.

(B.3)

Then there are Hilbert spaces
{\mathcal{K}}_{1},{\mathcal{K}}_{2}
and a unitary

W:{\mathcal{H}}\to{\mathcal{K}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{K}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits W\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}\right)={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{K}}_{1})\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{2}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2}},\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits W\left({\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}\right)={\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{K}}_{2})\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2}},\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits W(V_{1})=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{2}}\otimes\sigma_{z},\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits W(V_{2})=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{2}}\otimes\sigma_{x}.\end{split}

(B.4)

By Proposition 2.9 of
[
BO
]
(with
G
a trivial group),

{\mathcal{M}}_{i}^{(0)}

i=1,2
are type I factors.
By the assumption (
B.3
),

{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}
commute.
From (
B.3
) and the fact that
V_{i}
belongs to center of
{\mathcal{M}}_{i}
,
we know that
both of
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
commute with
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}
.
As a result, there are Hilbert spaces

{\mathcal{K}}_{1},{\mathcal{K}}_{2},{\mathcal{K}}_{3}
and a unitary
\hat{W}:{\mathcal{H}}\to{\mathcal{K}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{K}}_{2}\otimes{\mathcal{K}}_{3}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\hat{W}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{(0)}\right)={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{K}}_{1})\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{2}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{3}},\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\hat{W}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{(0)}\right)={\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{K}}_{2})\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{3}},\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\hat{W}(V_{1})=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{2}}\otimes y_{1},\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\hat{W}(V_{2})=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{2}}\otimes y_{2},\end{split}

(B.5)

with
y_{1},y_{2}
self-adjoint unitaries on
{\mathcal{K}}_{3}
.
Because
V_{2}V_{1}V_{2}^{*}=-V_{1}
,
we have
y_{2}y_{1}y_{2}^{*}=-y_{1}
.
With
y_{1}=r_{1+}-r_{1-}
as a spectral projection,
this means that
y_{2}r_{1\pm}y_{2}=r_{1\mp}
,
and
u:=r_{1+}y_{2}r_{1-}:r_{1-}{\mathcal{K}}_{3}\to r_{1+}{\mathcal{K}}_{3}

is a unitary.
Let
\{e_{1},e_{2}\}
be the standard basis of
{\mathbb{C}}^{2}

and set
v:{\mathcal{K}}_{3}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\otimes r_{1+}{\mathcal{K}}
be a unitary given
by

\displaystyle v\xi:=e_{1}\otimes r_{1+}\xi+e_{2}\otimes ur_{1-}\xi,\quad\xi\in{\mathcal{K}}_{3}.

(B.6)

It is then straightforward to show that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits v(y_{1})=\sigma_{z}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{r_{1+}{\mathcal{K}}},\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits v(y_{2})=\sigma_{x}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{r_{1+}{\mathcal{K}}}.\end{split}

(B.7)

From this, (
B.5
) and
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\vee{\mathcal{M}}_{2}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
,
we see that
r_{1+}{\mathcal{K}}
is one-dimensional.
Hence
W:=(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{2}}\otimes v)\hat{W}:{\mathcal{H}}\to{\mathcal{K}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{K}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{2}

defines a unitary
satisfying (
B.4
).

\square

Appendix C
Miscellaneous Lemmas

It is elementary to show the following Lemma. We omit the proof.

Lemma C.1
.

For
\sigma=L,R
,
let
{\mathcal{B}}_{\sigma}
be a graded
C^{*}
-algebra
with a grading automorphism
\Theta_{\sigma}
.
Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma},\pi_{\sigma})
be an irreducible representation of
{\mathcal{B}}_{\sigma}

with a self-adjoint unitary
\Gamma_{\sigma}
implementing

\Theta_{\sigma}
.
Let
\zeta_{\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits^{(0)}({\mathcal{B}}_{\sigma})
.
Then the followings hold.

(i)

If each
\zeta_{\sigma}
is implemented by a unitary
u_{\sigma}
on

({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma},\pi_{\sigma})
, i.e.,
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\sigma}\right)\circ\pi_{\sigma}=\pi_{\sigma}\circ\zeta_{\sigma}
,
then
u_{\sigma}
is homogeneous with respect to
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(\Gamma_{\sigma})
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes u_{R}\right)\circ\left(\pi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\right)=\left(\pi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\right)\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{B}}_{L}}\hat{\otimes}\zeta_{R}\right),\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{L}\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{\partial u_{L}}\right)\circ\left(\pi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\right)=\left(\pi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\right)\left(\zeta_{L}\hat{\otimes}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{B}}_{R}}\right).\end{split}

(C.1)

Here
\partial u_{L}
denotes the grade of
u_{L}
with respect to
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(\Gamma_{L})
.

(ii)

Suppose that there are unitaries
U_{\sigma}
,
\sigma=L,R
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{L}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{R}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{R}\right)\circ\left(\pi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\right)=\left(\pi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\right)\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{B}}_{L}}\hat{\otimes}\zeta_{R}\right),\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{L}\right)\circ\left(\pi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\right)=\left(\pi_{L}\hat{\otimes}\pi_{R}\right)\left(\zeta_{L}\hat{\otimes}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{B}}_{R}}\right).\end{split}

(C.2)

Then there are unitaries
u_{\sigma}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma})

such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\sigma}\right)\circ\pi_{\sigma}=\pi_{\sigma}\circ\zeta_{\sigma}
,
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\otimes u_{R}=U_{R},\quad u_{L}\otimes\Gamma_{R}^{\partial u_{L}}=U_{L}.

(C.3)
